# Nonuniqueness with local conservation for the hard-sphere Boltzmann equation

Coordinate `EN-109-000-0022-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-ML-H-0718` | `PM-363.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Nonuniqueness-with-local-conservation-for-the-hard-sphere-Boltzmann-equation-October-5-2026` | date 2026-10-05 | derived from the openai/math preprint Nonuniqueness-with-local-conservation-for-the-hard-sphere-Boltzmann-equation-October-5-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove nonuniqueness for the three-dimensional periodic hard-sphere Boltzmann equation among global entropy solutions satisfying exact local conservation of mass, momentum, and kinetic energy. We construct one nonnegative initial density with bounded velocity support and finite mass, energy, and absolute entropy that gives rise to two distinct such solutions. Both are strongly continuous in <i>L</i><sup>1</sup>, and their collision gain and loss terms are integrable with every polynomial velocity weight on every bounded time interval.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove nonuniqueness for the three-dimensional periodic hard-sphere Boltzmann equation among global entropy solutions satisfying exact local conservation of mass, momentum, and kinetic energy. We construct one nonnegative initial density with bounded velocity support and finite mass, energy, and absolute entropy that gives rise to two distinct such solutions. Both are strongly continuous in <i>L</i><sup>1</sup>, and their collision gain and loss terms are integrable with every polynomial velocity weight on every bounded time interval.

- **claim-conservative-nonuniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist $R<\infty$ and a nonnegative datum $F_0$ with $$\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}_vF_0\subset B_R(0),\qquad \int_\Omega F_0(1+\|v\|^2+\|\log F_0\|)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv<\infty,$$ for which <a href="#eq:collision" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:collision">[eq:collision]</a> has two global entropy solutions with local conservation in the sense of Definition <a href="#def:admissible" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:admissible">1</a>, differing on a set of positive $(t,x,v)$-measure. Both solutions belong to $C([0,\infty);L^1(\Omega))$. Moreover, for every finite $T$ and every $k\ge0$, they satisfy $$\label{eq:strong-collision-integrability} \int_0^T\!\!\int_\Omega \langle v\rangle^k\bigl[Q^+(F,F)+Q^-(F,F)\bigr] \,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv\,\mathrm dt<\infty, \qquad \langle v\rangle=(1+\|v\|^2)^{1/2}.$$
  - Explanation: A specially constructed datum admits distinct global entropy solutions with exact local conservation, strong time continuity, and separately integrable weighted collision gains and losses.
- **claim-carleman-collision-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-carleman)): For the normalization in <a href="#eq:collision" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:collision">[eq:collision]</a>, the symmetric bilinear gain is $$\label{ker:carleman} P(c,h)(v) =2\int_{\mathbb R^3}c(p)\,\,\mathrm dp \int_{\mathbb R^3} \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr)h(\xi)\,\,\mathrm d\xi.$$ For fixed $p\ne v$, the inner delta denotes surface measure on the plane $(v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)=0$, divided by $\|v-p\|$. For incoming velocities $p,\xi$, the outgoing measure has total mass $2\pi\|p-\xi\|$ and is uniform on the sphere with diameter $[p,\xi]$. Formula <a href="#ker:carleman" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:carleman">[ker:carleman]</a> also defines the gain when $c$ is a nonnegative velocity measure and $h$ is a nonnegative density, whenever the displayed integral is finite almost everywhere.
  - Explanation: The gain admits a symmetric collision-plane representation and an equivalent uniform diameter-sphere output law. The representation also accommodates a measure-valued cold input when its integral is finite almost everywhere.
- **claim-transverse-axial-slice-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-ker-geometric-slice)): Let $\lambda$ be a probability mixture of the measures $\mathcal U_d$, $d>0$, and $\delta_0$, and let $\rho$ be any axial probability measure. No bound on its density or its support is required. Put $c=\lambda\otimes\rho$. For $R\geq1$, $\bar v\ne0$, and an interval $I\subset\mathbb R$ of length $0<\ell\leq2R$, one has $$\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{W(v)}\int c(\,\mathrm dp) \int_{\|\xi-v\|\leq R} \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr)W(\xi)\,\,\mathrm d\xi \leq C_q R^2,\label{ker:geometric-total}\\ &\frac{1}{W(v)}\int c(\,\mathrm dp) \int_{\substack{\|\xi-v\|\leq R\\ \xi_3\in I}} \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr)W(\xi)\,\,\mathrm d\xi \leq C_q\bigl(R\ell+R^{q-1}\ell^{2-q}\bigr). \label{ker:geometric-interval} \end{aligned}$$ The constants are independent of all transverse widths and of $\rho$. In particular, these statements hold with $\lambda=\mathcal G_d$ for every $d\geq0$.
  - Explanation: Transverse disk averaging controls the total collision-plane integral and its restriction to an axial interval without imposing axial density or support bounds.
- **claim-uniform-sheet-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sheet-bound)): For every $L<\infty$ there is $C_{q,m,L}<\infty$ such that $$\label{ker:sheet-gain} P(c,H)(v)\leq C_{q,m,L}H(v) \quad\hbox{for almost every }v, \qquad c\in\mathcal S_L.$$ More quantitatively, for $R\geq1$, $$\label{ker:shell-tail} \mathcal K_c\bigl(v; R<\|p\|+\|\xi-v\|\leq2R\bigr) \leq C_{q,m,L}R^2e^{-cR^2},$$ where $c>0$ is an absolute constant. The same conclusions, multiplied by $A_0$, hold under sheet domination with amplitude $A_0$.
  - Explanation: Sheet domination gives a uniform weighted gain bound and a Gaussian shell tail, with linear dependence on the domination amplitude.
- **claim-sheet-kernel-tightness** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel-tightness)): Fix $1\leq L<\infty$. Uniformly for $c\in\mathcal S_L$ and outgoing velocities off the axis, the tails in $\|p\|+\|\xi-v\|$ are negligible: $$\label{ker:uniform-tail} \lim_{R\to\infty}\sup_{c\in\mathcal S_L}\mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_v \mathcal K_c(v;\|p\|+\|\xi-v\|>R)=0.$$ Moreover, $$\label{ker:large-output} \lim_{M\to\infty}\sup_{c\in\mathcal S_L} \mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{\|v\|>M}\mathcal K_c(v;\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3)=0.$$ Both conclusions hold under uniformly bounded sheet amplitudes.
  - Explanation: Normalized sheet kernels have uniformly negligible input tails, and their total mass vanishes at large outgoing speed.
- **claim-gaussian-parameter-sheet-domination** (bound, [source](#stmt-ker-parameter-sheets)): Let a measure in parameters $w=(\bar w,w_3)\in\mathbb R^2\times\mathbb R$ have density $j$ satisfying $$0\leq j(w)\leq C_0\pi^{-3/2}e^{-\|w\|^2}.$$ Let its velocity image be given by $$\label{ker:parameter-map} \bar p=d\bar w,\qquad p_3=\Phi(w_3),\qquad 0\leq d\leq\tfrac1{16},\qquad \|\Phi(a)\|\leq A+\delta\|a\|,\quad0\leq\delta\leq\tfrac1{16},$$ where $\Phi$ is merely measurable. The image measure is dominated by $C_0$ times a sheet in $\mathcal S_{L_A}$, with $L_A$ depending only on $A$. Its gain satisfies Lemma <a href="#lem:sheet-bound" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:sheet-bound">6</a> with a constant depending only on $q,m,A,C_0$.* *If $c_{>B}$ is the image of the restricted parameter measure $\mathbf 1_{\|w\|>B}j(w)\,\,\mathrm dw$, then $$\label{ker:parameter-tail} \mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_v\frac{P(c_{>B},H)(v)}{H(v)} \leq C_{q,m,A,C_0}e^{-B^2/2},\qquad B\geq0.$$ Thus parameter truncations are valid in the normalized collision measure, even when $\Phi$ compresses intervals or has atoms in its image law. Probability mixtures of measurable families with common $q,m,A,C_0$ and the width bounds in <a href="#ker:parameter-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:parameter-map">[ker:parameter-map]</a> preserve these assertions. More generally, a positive mixing measure of total mass $B_0<\infty$ multiplies the domination amplitude and quantitative upper bounds by $B_0$; the sheet parameter $L_A$ is unchanged. For <a href="#ker:parameter-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:parameter-tail">[ker:parameter-tail]</a>, impose the restriction $\|w\|>B$ in each component before mixing. Additional measurable masks taking values in $[0,1]$ preserve the assertions.
  - Explanation: A Gaussian parameter majorant remains sheet dominated under measurable axial compression. Parameter tails, bounded masks, and finite positive mixtures preserve the estimates with the stated amplitude dependence.
- **claim-quadratic-hot-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-hot-bound)): There is a constant $C_{q,m}$ such that $$\label{ker:hot-gain} P(H,H)\leq C_{q,m}H \quad\hbox{almost everywhere}.$$ In fact the proof below works for every $m>5$.
  - Explanation: The gain of the singular velocity majorant against itself stays below a fixed multiple of that majorant.
- **claim-small-mass-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-gain-small-mass)): For each $0\leq B<\infty$ and $\varepsilon>0$ there is a finite constant $C_{B,\varepsilon}$ such that every measurable density $j$ with $0\leq j\leq BH$ satisfies $$\label{ker:gain-small-mass} P(H,j)(v)\leq H(v) \left(\varepsilon+C_{B,\varepsilon} \int_{\mathbb R^3}j(p)\,\,\mathrm dp\right) \qquad\hbox{for almost every }v.$$ The same constant works with $P^b$ in place of $P$.
  - Explanation: Small velocity mass makes the normalized gain small within a fixed weighted order interval. The domination hypothesis is essential, and the same estimate applies to the collision cutoff.
- **claim-irrational-lattice-separation** (bound, [source](#stmt-con-lattice)): There is a numerical constant $c>0$ such that $$\|\bar n\|\ge\frac{c}{1+\|n\|}\qquad (n\in\Lambda\setminus\{0\}). \label{con:diophantine}$$
  - Explanation: The rotated lattice has a quantitative transverse separation from the jet axis, which later prevents premature intersections of periodic copies.
- **claim-datum-integrability-and-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-con-datum)): The density $F_0$ is nonnegative, has bounded velocity support, and satisfies $$\int F_0(1+\|v\|^2+\|\log F_0\|)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv<\infty.$$ Its truncations with $j\le N$, with or without a seed <a href="#con:seed" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="con:seed">[con:seed]</a>, converge to $F_0$ in weighted $L^1$ for every fixed polynomial velocity weight and in relative entropy with respect to either $M$ or $\mu$. The convergence is uniform for $0<T\le t_1$ and $0\le\lambda\le L_0$. Moreover their initial $\mu$-relative entropy can be made arbitrarily small, uniformly in $N,T,\lambda$, by first decreasing $\delta$ and increasing $R_\mu$, then decreasing $s_0$.
  - Explanation: The jet datum has bounded velocity support and finite mass, energy, and absolute entropy. Truncations and vanishing seeds converge in weighted mass and relative entropy, with small initial relative entropy obtained in the prescribed order of parameter choices.
- **claim-finite-colored-evolution** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-con-finite-evolution)): For fixed $N,b,T,\lambda$, the colored equations have a unique nonnegative local mild solution with bounded total $H$-norm. It continues while that norm remains bounded, with the prescribed transfers at retirement times, and depends continuously in this norm on its initial data on every common bounded interval of existence. The norm of each component is continuous between transfers.* *On every such interval, total mass, momentum, and energy are conserved. The exact local collision-invariant balances hold, with all polynomial velocity fluxes integrable. For $\rho=M$ or $\rho=\mu$, one has at every time of existence $$\mathcal H_\rho(F(t))+ \int_0^t\mathcal D^b(F(s))\,\mathrm ds \le\mathcal H_\rho(F(0)), \label{con:finite-entropy}$$ where $\mathcal D^b$ is the entropy dissipation with the same cutoff.
  - Explanation: Each fixed finite cutoff system has a unique local mild evolution with a weighted-norm continuation criterion and continuous dependence. Its total density satisfies exact local balances and the cutoff entropy inequality.
- **claim-active-tube-disjointness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cold-geometry)): For sufficiently small $s_0$, the sets $\mathcal O_i(t)$ for active labels are pairwise disjoint in the periodic lift. More precisely, $\mathcal O_i(t)\cap\mathcal O_{i'}(t)=\varnothing$ for distinct labels whenever $t\le\min\{L_{s_i},L_{s_{i'}}\}$. Also $$\widehat E_j^\sigma\subset\{s_j<\|y\|<e^\eta s_j\}.$$
  - Explanation: Active spatial enlargements remain pairwise disjoint until the earlier retirement deadline, including across distinct periodic copies.
- **claim-cold-barrier-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-cold-barrier)): A fixed sufficiently large $C_m$ gives, for $0\le t\le L_s$, $$\begin{aligned} f_i&\le m_i,\qquad D_tm_i\ge \frac1{L_s} \left(1+\frac78\frac{\|\xi\|^2}{d_s^2}\right)m_i, \label{cold:barrier-derivative}\\ P(m_i,m_i)&=m_i\nu_{m_i} \le C\frac K{s}(d_s+\|\xi\|)m_i. \label{cold:barrier-gain} \end{aligned}$$ The differential inequality holds almost everywhere along flights. For sufficiently small $s_0$, $f_i(t,x,v)>0$ implies $x\in\mathcal O_i(t)$ throughout the same time interval.
  - Explanation: A Gaussian barrier dominates the transported core, has a favorable transport derivative, and controls its self-gain. The transported support stays inside the corresponding spatial enlargement.
- **claim-active-color-control-and-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-cold-control)): There is a fixed constant $C_*$ such that, with $$\epsilon_s=C_*L_sd_s/s,\qquad \mathcal U_i=f_i+\epsilon_s m_i,$$ and sufficiently small $s_0$, every finite colored evolution obeys $$0\le c_i(t,x,v)\le\mathcal U_i(t,x,v)\qquad (0\le t\le L_{s_i}) \label{cold:upper}$$ as long as that color is active and the solution exists. If, on an interval beginning at $t_1$, one also has $\nu_F^b\ge\kappa>0$, then $$c_i(t,x,v)\le e^{-\kappa(t-t_1)}\mathcal U_i(t,x,v)\qquad (t_1\le t\le L_{s_i}) \label{cold:decay}$$ throughout that interval. None of the constants in <a href="#cold:upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cold:upper">[cold:upper]</a> uses a bound on the bath or hot density.
  - Explanation: Every active color is bounded by its free core plus a small barrier remainder independently of the hot and bath densities. A positive collision-frequency lower bound adds exponential decay.
- **claim-core-jacobian-and-sheet-amplitude** (bound, [source](#stmt-cold-core-amplitude)): At a fixed $(t,x)$, the velocity measure $f_i(t,x,p)\,\mathrm dp$ is the pushforward of $$\frac{K S(\gamma(z_c-tp_3)/s)} {s+t\alpha\gamma S(\gamma(z_c-tp_3)/s)}\,J(w)\,\mathrm dw,\qquad \bar p=d_s\bar w,\quad p_3=a_s^\sigma(z_c-tp_3)+d_sw_3, \label{cold:core-formula}$$ with the initial spatial masks evaluated at $x-tp$. For $t\le L_s$, it is dominated by probability sheets with total amplitude $C/(t+s)$. The remainder $\epsilon_s m_i\,\mathrm dp$ has sheet amplitude at most $$C L_sd_s/s^2\le Cs^{15/2}\le C/(t+s). \label{cold:remainder-amplitude}$$ Thus the full upper bound $\mathcal U_i\,\mathrm dp$ has sheet amplitude $C/(t+s)$. In particular, for every fixed $k\ge0$, a main color satisfies $$\int\langle p\rangle^k c_i(t,x,p)\,\mathrm dp\le\frac{C_k}{t+s},\qquad \int_{\|p\|>R}\langle p\rangle^k c_i(t,x,p)\,\mathrm dp \le\frac{C_k e^{-c_kR^2}}{t+s}\quad(R\ge3). \label{cold:moments}$$
  - Explanation: The exact axial Jacobian yields a sheet amplitude controlled by the inverse of time plus scale. The barrier remainder obeys the same envelope, giving polynomial moments and Gaussian velocity tails.
- **claim-nonmain-gaussian-tails** (bound, [source](#stmt-cold-nonmain)): There is a function $\theta_0(s_0)\to0$ as $s_0\downarrow0$ such that, uniformly at all times of existence, $$\sum_{\substack{i\ \mathrm{active}\\x\notin\mathcal O_i(t)}} \mathcal U_i(t,x,v) \le\theta_0(s_0)e^{-4\|v\|^2}. \label{cold:nonmain-bound}$$ For $0<t\le1$, and any fixed $A,B>0$, the coefficient can instead be taken to be $C_{A,B}t^A s_0^B$. Both assertions remain valid for an arbitrary subset of the active labels.
  - Explanation: Summed nonmain tails admit a Gaussian majorant whose coefficient vanishes with the base scale. On the early interval that coefficient gains arbitrary fixed powers of time and scale.
- **claim-retirement-size-and-tube-volume** (bound, [source](#stmt-cold-retirement-size)): For some fixed constants $C,C'>0$, $$\left\\|\sum_{i:\,s_i=s}\mathcal U_i(L_s)\right\\|_H \le C s^{-C'}. \label{cold:retirement-bound}$$ Moreover, uniformly over the active label sets and all times, $$\|\mathcal O(t)\|\le\sum_{j\ge1}C s_j^2(1+L_{s_j}) \le C s_0^{3/2}. \label{cold:tube-volume}$$
  - Explanation: The retirement norm grows at most polynomially in inverse scale, while the total volume of active tubes tends to zero with the base scale.
- **claim-bath-hole-barrier** (bound, [source](#stmt-early-bath)): There are $t_1\in(0,1/32]$, $\beta'>4$, and constants $C,c>0$, independent of $\delta$, $R_\mu$, and $s_0$, such that every finite evolution of <a href="#con:colors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="con:colors">[con:colors]</a> satisfies, on its interval of existence, $$\label{early:bath-bound} 0\le u(t,x,v)\le C e^{-\beta'\|v\|^2} \exp\!\left(-\frac{cD_0(x)^2}{t}\right), \qquad 0<t\le t_1, \qquad D_0(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,D_{\rm hole}^c).$$ For sufficiently small $s_0$ depending on $\delta$, one also has $$\label{early:hole-distance} D_0(x)\ge\delta \qquad (0\le t\le t_1,\ x\in\mathcal O(t)).$$
  - Explanation: The bath has Gaussian velocity decay and exponentially small penetration into the initial spatial hole. Active tubes remain a fixed positive distance inside that hole during the early interval.
- **claim-early-cross-source-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-early-source-estimates)): For sufficiently small $s_0$ depending on $\delta$, every finite evolution satisfies $$\label{early:source} P(c+u,H)\le\frac{C}{t+s_N}H, \qquad 0\le\mathcal S\le C_{A,\delta}t^{A-1}H, \qquad 0<t\le t_1,$$ on its interval of existence, for every fixed $A>0$. Here $C$ is independent of $\delta$ and $R_\mu$, and the constants are uniform for all sufficiently small $s_0$. No bound on $h$ is assumed.
  - Explanation: Cold and bath gains have the inverse time-plus-smallest-scale envelope, while cross-color and cold-bath sources vanish to arbitrarily high fixed order near the initial time without any hot bound.
- **claim-cap-selection** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-early-cap-selection)): For fixed $\delta>0$, there are $\bar s_0,T_0>0$, uniform in $N$ and the collision cutoff, with the following property. For each $0<s_0\le\bar s_0$, each $0<T\le T_0$, and each finite approximation, a seed parameter $\lambda\in(0,L_0)$ gives a solution on $[0,T]$ satisfying $$\label{early:cap} Z(t)\le m_T(t+s_N)\quad(0\le t\le T), \qquad \max_{0\le t\le T}\frac{Z(t)}{m_T(t+s_N)}=1.$$ The dormant solution exists on a fixed initial interval and satisfies $$\label{early:dormant-bound} Z(t)\le C'_{A,\delta}t^A.$$ The interval and constant in <a href="#early:dormant-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="early:dormant-bound">[early:dormant-bound]</a> are independent of $N$, the collision cutoff, and sufficiently small $s_0$.
  - Explanation: Continuous variation of the hot seed selects an evolution that stays below the prescribed cap and attains it. The dormant evolution remains smaller by a uniform early-time power bound.
- **claim-small-source-along-flights** (bound, [source](#stmt-early-path-source)): Fix $0<t_b<t_1$. There is a function $E_{t_b}(s_0)$ tending to zero as $s_0\to0$ such that, for every finite evolution and every terminal time $t\in[t_b,t_1]$ in its interval of existence, $$\label{early:source-integral} \left\\|\int_{t_b}^t \mathcal S(s,x-(t-s)v,v)\,\mathrm ds\right\\|_H \le E_{t_b}(s_0).$$ This estimate is uniform in the hot seed, the cap horizon, the approximation index, and the collision cutoff.
  - Explanation: After a fixed positive lower time, the cross-collision source has a uniformly small integral along free flights as the base jet scale shrinks.
- **claim-uniform-early-continuation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-early-continuation)): Fix $\delta>0$ and $\zeta\in(0,1)$. There are $\bar s_0>0$ and $T_0\in(0,t_1)$, depending on $\delta$ and $\zeta$ and on the fixed jet parameters, such that for every $0<s_0\le\bar s_0$, every finite approximation, and every $0<T\le T_0$, the dormant solution and every solution selected in Proposition <a href="#early:cap-selection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="early:cap-selection">22</a> continue to $t_1$ and satisfy $$\label{early:small-hot} \sup_{0\le t\le t_1}\\|h(t)\\|_H\le\zeta.$$ The choices are uniform in $N$, the collision cutoff, $R_\mu$, and $T$. For dormant solutions, <a href="#early:dormant-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="early:dormant-bound">[early:dormant-bound]</a> also holds on an initial interval independent of these indices, with the fixed exponent $A>p+1$ chosen in <a href="#early:exponents" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="early:exponents">[early:exponents]</a>.
  - Explanation: Both dormant and selected cap evolutions reach the fixed bath time with uniformly small hot weighted norm. The parameter choices are independent of the approximation and cap horizon.
- **claim-flight-averaging** (bound, [source](#stmt-global-flight-average)): Let $A:[0,\infty)\times(\mathbb R^3/\Lambda)\to[0,\infty)$ be measurable, write $A_s=A(s,\cdot)$, and suppose $$\sup_s\\|A_s\\|_\infty\le A_*,\qquad \sup_s\\|A_s\\|_1\le a_*.$$ For a fixed bounded velocity set $V$ and $\rho>0$, $$\label{global:ball-average} \int_V A_s(x-\tau v)\,\mathrm dv \le C_{V,\rho}a_* \qquad(\tau\ge\rho),$$ uniformly in $x,s,\tau$. If $A_*$ is fixed, the following quantities tend to zero as $a_*\to0$: $$\begin{aligned} &\sup_{t\ge t_1,x} \int_{t_1}^t e^{-\kappa(t-s)} \int H(v)A_s(x-(t-s)v)\,\mathrm dv\,\mathrm ds, \label{global:damped-average}\\ &\sup_{t\ge t_1,x} \int_0^{t_1}\int H(v)A_s(x-(t-s)v)\,\mathrm dv\,\mathrm ds, \label{global:early-average} \end{aligned}$$ where $\kappa>0$ and $t_1>0$ are fixed. The supremum in $x$ is essential.
  - Explanation: Uniformly bounded spatial densities with small spatial integral have small velocity averages along flights after a time deletion. Weighted damped and early-time integrals become small uniformly over later endpoints.
- **claim-bootstrap-independent-frequency** (bound, [source](#stmt-global-frequency)): There is a constant $\kappa>0$, depending only on the fixed Maxwellian $\mu$ and $t_1$, with the following property. For each fixed $B>B_0+2$, if $\varepsilon_E$ is sufficiently small depending on $B$, then every finite evolution satisfying <a href="#global:bootstrap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="global:bootstrap">[global:bootstrap]</a> obeys $$\label{global:frequency-bound} \nu_F^b(t,x,v)\ge\kappa\qquad(t\ge t_1).$$ The constant is uniform for $b\ge1$ and for all approximation indices.
  - Explanation: Sufficiently small entropy distance supplies a positive collision-frequency lower bound after the bath time. The damping constant is fixed independently of the provisional weighted bound.
- **claim-bootstrap-independent-nonlinear-gain** (bound, [source](#stmt-global-gain)): Let $B>B_0+2$ be fixed and suppose <a href="#global:bootstrap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="global:bootstrap">[global:bootstrap]</a> holds on an interval beginning at $t_1$. After taking $\varepsilon_E$, $\zeta$, and $s_0$ sufficiently small depending on $B$, one has on that interval $$\label{global:gain-bound} P^b(w,w)\le C_5H, \qquad C_5:=\\|P(\mu,\mu)\\|_H+1.$$ In particular, $C_5$ is independent of $B$, the approximation, and the cap horizon. The estimate holds also at the appropriate one-sided sections of retirement times.
  - Explanation: Within the weighted bootstrap interval, flight averaging makes the excess velocity mass small enough to bound the nonlinear gain by a constant independent of the bootstrap size.
- **claim-global-finite-continuation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-global-control)): The construction parameters can be fixed so that every dormant finite evolution and every cap solution with $0<T\le T_0$ continues globally. There are constants $B<\infty$ and $\kappa>0$, independent of $N$, $b_N\ge1$, and $T$, such that $$\label{global:uniform-bound} \\|w(t)\\|_H\le B,\qquad \nu_F^{b_N}(t,x,v)\ge\kappa, \qquad t\ge t_1.$$ Every active jet satisfies <a href="#global:cold-decay" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="global:cold-decay">[global:cold-decay]</a> through its retirement, the total retirement increment is bounded by <a href="#global:retirement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="global:retirement">[global:retirement]</a>, and the hot component satisfies the small bound of <a href="#early:continuation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="early:continuation">24</a> on $[0,t_1]$.
  - Explanation: An ordered choice of parameters closes the global weighted bootstrap across every retirement. Both finite families continue globally with uniform damping and a bounded merged component.
- **claim-global-conservative-limits** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-global-limits)): Fix the construction parameters supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:global-control" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:global-control">28</a>, and let $b_N\to\infty$. Either the dormant family, or a family of cap solutions at one fixed horizon $T\in(0,T_0]$, has a subsequence of total densities $F_N$ converging weakly in $L^1([0,\tau]\times\Omega)$ for every $\tau<\infty$ to a nonnegative density $F$. This subsequence can be chosen so that $F_N(t)\rightharpoonup F(t)$ in $L^1(\Omega)$ at every $t\ge0$, with uniformly small polynomially weighted velocity tails on each bounded time interval.* *The limit has initial datum $F_0$ and is a global entropy solution obeying local conservation in the sense of Definition <a href="#def:admissible" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:admissible">1</a>. More precisely, $$\label{comp:strong-properties} F\in C([0,\infty);L^1(\Omega)),\qquad \int_0^\tau\!\int_\Omega \langle v\rangle^k\bigl(F+Q^+(F,F)+Q^-(F,F)\bigr) \,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv\,\mathrm dt<\infty \quad(k\ge0,\ \tau<\infty).$$ Total mass, momentum, and energy are conserved at every time. The common early-time pointwise majorant of the dormant approximations also bounds any dormant limit.
  - Explanation: Removing jet truncation and collision cutoff produces global entropy limits with strong time traces and exact local balances. Separate weighted collision integrability prevents conservation defects.
- **claim-uniformly-integrable-velocity-averaging** (theorem, [source](#stmt-comp-averaging)): Let $f_n,g_n$ be locally uniformly integrable in $L^1$ on an open region of time, space, and velocity, and suppose that $(\partial_t+v\cdot\nabla_x)f_n=g_n$ there in distributions. For every smooth compactly supported velocity test $\psi$, the averages $\int\psi(v)f_n(t,x,v)\,\mathrm dv$ are relatively compact in local $L^1_{t,x}$ wherever the hypotheses hold for all velocities in a neighborhood of $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\psi$. The spatial domain may be a flat torus or an open subset of $\mathbb R^3$.
  - Explanation: Local uniform integrability of a transported sequence and its source yields local strong compactness of smooth compactly supported velocity averages.
- **claim-cap-dilation-linear-limit** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-blowup-limit)): Every sequence <a href="#eq:rescaling" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rescaling">[eq:rescaling]</a> has a subsequence for which $h_{r_N}\rightharpoonup g$ in local spacetime $L^1$ on $(0,U)\times\mathbb R^3_x\times\mathbb R^3_v$, where $$\label{eq:limit-equation} 0\leq g(t,x,v)\leq(t+l)H(v),\qquad D_tg=2P(B,g)-g\nu_B.$$ Here $B$ is <a href="#eq:background" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:background">[eq:background]</a>; its gain and loss are defined by integration against the velocity measure. Both terms on the right are locally integrable, with a velocity majorant uniform in position on each compact positive-time interval. Compactly supported velocity averages of $h_{r_N}$ converge strongly in local $L^1_{t,x}$. The limit has a locally strongly continuous $L^1$ representative at positive times and obeys the integrating-factor formula <a href="#dil:mild" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dil:mild">[dil:mild]</a> below.* *If $l>0$, pass also to a limit $\lambda_N\to\lambda_0$ of the seed parameters. The conclusion and its local continuity extend to time zero, with $$\label{dil:initial-trace} g(0,x,v)=\lambda_0 l\,\chi(x/l,v).$$
  - Explanation: Cap dilation has a subsequential linear kinetic limit in an axial measure-valued medium. Velocity averages converge strongly, positive-time traces satisfy a mild formula, and a positive lower mesh endpoint also gives an initial trace.
- **claim-free-core-upper-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-dil-free-core-comparison)): For all sufficiently large $N$, define the velocity measure $$A_N(t,x,\,\mathrm dp) =r_N\sum_{i:\,j\le N}f_i(r_Nt,r_Nx,p)\,\mathrm dp, \qquad 0\le t\le1.$$ This definition uses free transport of the initial colors and is meaningful even if $t>U_N$. At each position, at most one summand is nonzero. Its velocity moments and normalized gain obey $$\int\langle p\rangle^k A_N(t,x,\,\mathrm dp) \le\frac{C_k}{t+l_N},\qquad P(A_N,H)\le\frac{C}{t+l_N}H.$$ There are numbers $\varepsilon_N\downarrow0$ such that, on $0\le t\le\min(1,U_N)$, the hot component obeys the pathwise upper comparison $$\label{dil:positive-comparison} D_th_{r_N}\le2P(A_N,h_{r_N})+\varepsilon_NH.$$ The bounds and comparison hold on full-measure endpoint sets at each time section, with representatives given by their path formulas.
  - Explanation: Explicit transported cores define a majorizing collision measure independently of the cap interval. Their gain bounds give a pathwise hot upper comparison with a vanishing error.
- **claim-cap-limit-nonvanishing** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-nonzero-limit)): Consider any sequence of cap solutions with horizons $T_n\to0$, approximation indices $N_n\to\infty$, and $s_{N_n}/T_n\to0$. Make the rescaling <a href="#eq:rescaling" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rescaling">[eq:rescaling]</a>, and pass to any subsequence supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:blowup-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:blowup-limit">31</a>. Its limit satisfies $$g\not\equiv0\qquad\text{on }(0,U)\times\mathbb R^3_x\times\mathbb R^3_v.$$ This conclusion includes $l=1$, $U=1$, and $U=\infty$.
  - Explanation: Every extracted cap limit is nonzero, including endpoint mesh and horizon cases. Attained weighted size cannot disappear through concentration or escape.
- **claim-dilated-core-kernel-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-nc-core-kernels)): There are nonnegative velocity measures $A_n(t,x,\,\mathrm dp)$ and numbers $\varepsilon_n\to0$ with the following properties. After discarding finitely many terms, the measures $A_n$ are defined from free transport on the whole interval $0\le t\le1$, independently of the cap interval $[0,U_n]$. The bounds on $A_n$ below hold on that whole interval; comparison with $h_n$ is used only while the cap bound holds.* 1. *On $0<t<\min(1,U_n)$, along almost every free characteristic, $$\label{nc:positive-equation} D_th_n\le 2P(A_n,h_n)+\varepsilon_n H .$$ At each point $A_n$ contains at most one main free core. Its dilated scale $\varrho=s/r_n$ satisfies $\|y\|\asymp\varrho$, with coordinates measured from its periodic copy, and $$\frac{2P(A_n,H)}{H}\le\frac{C}{t+\ell_n}.$$* 2. *Before spatial masks are imposed, a core of scale $\varrho=s/r_n$ and sign $\sigma$ is the pushforward of $$\label{nc:core-parameters} \frac{K S\bigl(\gamma(z-tp_3)/\varrho\bigr)} {\varrho+t\alpha\gamma S\bigl(\gamma(z-tp_3)/\varrho\bigr)} J(w)\,\mathrm dw , \qquad \bar p=d_s\bar w,\quad p_3=\sigma+\alpha\tanh\bigl(\gamma(z-tp_3)/\varrho\bigr)+d_sw_3 .$$ The actual masks are measurable factors between zero and one. The density in <a href="#nc:core-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="nc:core-parameters">[nc:core-parameters]</a> is at most $C(t+\varrho)^{-1}J(w)$.* 3. *For $t\ge a>0$, the normalized gain measure $$\label{nc:normalized-kernel} \mathcal K_n(t,x,v;\,\mathrm dp\,\,\mathrm d\xi) = 4A_n(t,x,\,\mathrm dp)\, \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr) \frac{H(\xi)}{H(v)}\,\mathrm d\xi$$ has total mass at most $C/a$. Uniformly in its base point, the contributions from large $\|p\|+\|\xi-v\|$, or from large retained Gaussian parameters in a product-sheet majorant, tend to zero. Its total mass tends uniformly to zero as $\|v\|\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: Free cores supply a positive majorizing equation, exact compressed parameter laws, and normalized kernels with uniform tail control on positive-time slabs.
- **claim-gain-endpoint-tightness** (bound, [source](#stmt-nc-endpoint-tightness)): Fix $a>0$, and let $t_n\in[a,1]$. In a five-fold integral $(\mathcal V_n^5F_n)(t_n,x_n,v_n)$, assume $0\le F_n\le2$. For every prescribed error, one may restrict all $\|p_i\|+\|v_i-v_{i-1}\|$ and sheet parameters to fixed bounded sets at that error, uniformly in $n,t_n,x_n,v_n$. Moreover:* 1. *if $\|v_n\|\to\infty$, the whole integral tends to zero;* 2. *after retaining bounded endpoint velocities, an endpoint escaping every bounded neighborhood of every dilated periodic origin also has a vanishing integral.* *Consequently any sequence along which the five-fold integral has a positive lower bound has, after periodic translations and subselection, convergent endpoint positions and velocities.
  - Explanation: Positive iterated gain contributions can be restricted to bounded velocity increments and parameters. Their endpoints stay compact after periodic translation.
- **claim-axis-kernel-controls** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-axis-kernel-estimates)): Fix $1\leq L<\infty$. The following conclusions hold uniformly for $c\in\mathcal S_L$ and for outgoing velocities off the axis.* 1. *For every fixed $M,\delta>0$, $$\begin{aligned} &\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0} \sup_{c\in\mathcal S_L} \mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{\substack{\|v\|\leq M\\0<\|\bar v\|\leq\varepsilon}} \mathcal K_c(v;\|\bar\xi\|\geq\delta)=0, \label{ker:axis-input}\\ &\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0} \sup_{c\in\mathcal S_L} \mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{\substack{\|v\|\leq M\\0<\|\bar v\|\leq\varepsilon}} \mathcal K_c(v;\|v_3-p_3\|\geq\delta)=0. \label{ker:axis-mismatch} \end{aligned}$$* 2. *The axial input marginals are uniformly non-atomic. More precisely, for each $R\geq1$ and each interval $I$ of length $0<\ell\leq1$, $$\label{ker:axial-interval} \mathcal K_c(v;\|p\|+\|\xi-v\|\leq R,\ \xi_3\in I) \leq C_{q,m,L,R}\bigl(\ell+\ell^{2-q}\bigr).$$ In combination with <a href="#ker:uniform-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ker:uniform-tail">[ker:uniform-tail]</a>, this gives $$\label{ker:axial-modulus} \lim_{\ell\downarrow0}\sup_{c\in\mathcal S_L}\mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_v \sup_{\|I\|\leq\ell}\mathcal K_c(v;\xi_3\in I)=0.$$* 3. *Let $\mathcal S_L(\varepsilon)$ denote the subclass whose elementary transverse widths are at most $\varepsilon$. For every $M<\infty$ and $r_0>0$, $$\label{ker:off-axis-small-input} \lim_{\delta\downarrow0}\limsup_{\varepsilon\downarrow0} \sup_{c\in\mathcal S_L(\varepsilon)} \mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{\substack{\|v\|\leq M\\\|\bar v\|\geq r_0}} \mathcal K_c(v;\|\bar\xi\|<\delta)=0.$$* *All these conclusions hold under uniformly bounded sheet amplitudes.
  - Explanation: Near-axis outputs concentrate transverse inputs and suppress axial mismatch, while axial input marginals remain uniformly nonatomic. Away from the axis, narrow cold sheets suppress near-axis hot inputs.
- **claim-characteristic-speed-matching** (bound, [source](#stmt-nc-characteristic-matching)): On every compact set with $t\ge a>0$, the following statements hold uniformly.* 1. *If $a\le t_2\le t_1$, $\Delta=t_1-t_2$, and $z_2=z_1-\Delta w$, then $$\label{nc:matching-inequality} \|w-u_{\varrho,\sigma}(t_1,z_1)\| \le \|w-u_{\varrho,\sigma}(t_2,z_2)\|.$$* 2. *As $\varrho\downarrow0$, the distance of $u_{\varrho,\sigma}(t,z)$ to $$\{z/t,\ \sigma-\alpha,\ \sigma+\alpha\}$$ tends to zero uniformly. For the perturbed axial velocity in <a href="#nc:core-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="nc:core-parameters">[nc:core-parameters]</a>, $$\label{nc:thermal-speed-error} \|p_3-u_{\varrho,\sigma}(t,z)\|\le d_s\|w_3\|.$$
  - Explanation: Axial profile mismatch cannot decrease backward along the relevant characteristic. Small scales approach a finite list of collapsed speeds, and the thermal perturbation stays controlled by its parameter displacement.
- **claim-two-gain-axial-vanishing** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-nc-two-axial-gains)): Fix $a>0$. Let $t_n\in[a,1]$, and suppose the endpoint positions and velocities are bounded with $\bar v_n\to0$. Then $$(\mathcal V_n^2 1)(t_n,x_n,v_n)\longrightarrow0.$$ The conclusion continues to hold if all subsequent factors in a longer finite gain integral are bounded uniformly.
  - Explanation: Successive gains vanish at bounded endpoints whose velocities approach the axis. Uniformly bounded later factors preserve this conclusion.
- **claim-redundant-plane-parameters** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nc-plane-parameters)): Let $n\ne0$, $e=n/\|n\|$, and let $\zeta$ be a bounded nonnegative function supported in $[-1,1]$ with integral one. For every nonnegative measurable $F$, $$\label{nc:plane-identity} \int_{n^\perp} F(k)\frac{\,\mathrm dA(k)}{\|n\|} = \int_{\mathbb R^3}F(n\times L)\|n\|\zeta(L\cdot e)\,\mathrm dL .$$ When $\delta\le\|n\|\le R$ and the plane integral is restricted to $\|k\|\le R_1$, the corresponding parameter measure $\mathbf 1_{\{\|n\times L\|\le R_1\}}\|n\|\zeta(L\cdot e)\,\mathrm dL$ is supported in $$\|L\|\le\sqrt{(R_1/\delta)^2+1}$$ and has density at most $R\\|\zeta\\|_\infty$. This bound concerns the parameter measure before multiplication by $F(n\times L)$. The identity can be applied successively when each normal is a measurable function of parameters chosen at earlier gains.
  - Explanation: A redundant volume parameterization replaces each collision-plane integral while retaining bounded parameter support and density after normal and increment truncations.
- **claim-uniform-submersion-pullback** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nc-uniform-pullback)): Let $\mathcal O\subset\mathbb R^k$ be open, $k\ge d$, and suppose $\Phi_n,\Phi:\mathcal O\to\mathbb R^d$ satisfy $\Phi_n\to\Phi$ in $C^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$ and $\operatorname{rank}D\Phi=d$ almost everywhere. Let $K\Subset\mathcal O$ and $B\subset\mathbb R^d$ be compact. Let $a_n$ be measurable, supported in $K$, with $\|a_n\|\le A$. Let $F_n$ be measurable, supported in $B$, with $\|F_n\|\le M$, and converging to zero in measure. Then $$\label{nc:pullback-conclusion} \int_{\mathcal O}\|F_n(\Phi_n(q))a_n(q)\|\,\mathrm dq\longrightarrow0 .$$ No convergence or continuity of the densities $a_n$ is required.
  - Explanation: Bounded functions vanishing in measure have vanishing weighted pullbacks under locally convergent smooth maps whose limiting differential has full rank almost everywhere. Parameter densities need only be bounded and measurable.
- **claim-five-gain-full-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-five-gain-rank)): Fix a nonaxial endpoint velocity $v_0$, an endpoint $(t_0,x_0)$ with $t_0>0$, five positive scales $\varrho_i$, and five signs $\sigma_i$. Given positive times $t_i$ and $L_i\in\mathbb R^3$, define recursively $$\label{nc:rank-map} \begin{split} x_i&=x_{i-1}-(t_{i-1}-t_i)v_{i-1},\\ p_i&=u_{\varrho_i,\sigma_i}(t_i,z_i)e_3,\qquad n_i=v_{i-1}-p_i,\\ k_i&=n_i\times L_i,\qquad v_i=v_{i-1}+k_i , \qquad i=1,\ldots,5 . \end{split}$$ The map $$\Phi:(t_1,\ldots,t_5,L_1,\ldots,L_5) \longmapsto(t_5,x_5,v_5)$$ is real analytic on the connected open set $(0,\infty)^5\times\mathbb R^{15}$, and has rank seven almost everywhere. In particular it is a submersion almost everywhere on the ordered region $0<t_5<\cdots<t_1<t_0$.* *For convergent endpoint sequences with a nonaxial limiting velocity, convergent positive scale sequences, and the finite-temperature core formulas <a href="#nc:core-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="nc:core-parameters">[nc:core-parameters]</a>, the analogous maps converge in $C^1$ on compact parameter sets to $\Phi$. Bounded measurable parameter densities supported in a common compact set therefore satisfy Lemma <a href="#nc:uniform-pullback" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nc:uniform-pullback">41</a>, including after any measurable spatial, velocity, or time restrictions.
  - Explanation: The iterated collision parameter map is analytic and has full spacetime-velocity rank almost everywhere at nonaxial endpoints. Finite-temperature maps converge smoothly enough for uniform pullback estimates.
- **claim-five-gain-nonaxial-vanishing** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-nc-five-nonaxial-gains)): Suppose $\ell_n\to0$, the hit endpoints converge after periodic translation, and their limiting velocity is nonaxial. Suppose also that $0\le f_n\le2$ on $a\le t\le\tau_n$ and $f_n\to0$ in measure on every compact subset of $$(0,U)\times\mathbb R^3_x \times\{v\in\mathbb R^3:\bar v\ne0\}.$$ Then $$(\mathcal V_n^5f_n)(\tau_n,x_n,v_n)\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: At compact nonaxial hit endpoints, the iterated gain integral vanishes when the bounded input sequence vanishes locally in measure away from the velocity axis.
- **claim-finite-dilated-horizon** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-dilated-horizon)): There are fixed choices $$K\ge1,\qquad 0<\eta\le\log2,\qquad 0<\alpha\le\tfrac14,\qquad 0<\gamma\le1$$ with the following property. For every allowed geometric mesh with lower endpoint $l\in[0,1]$, the only nonnegative density solving <a href="#eq:limit-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-equation">[eq:limit-equation]</a> on $0<t<\infty$, with locally integrable gain and loss, the positive-time mild formula <a href="#dil:mild" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dil:mild">[dil:mild]</a>, and the bound $g(t,x,v)\le(t+l)H(v)$, is zero. Consequently every nonzero limit in Propositions <a href="#prop:blowup-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:blowup-limit">31</a> and <a href="#prop:nonzero-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:nonzero-limit">33</a> has $U<\infty$. The four parameters can be fixed before the thermal, seed, bath, and final horizon parameters of the construction. Their choice is independent of the mesh phase and of $l$.
  - Explanation: Fixed jet parameters exclude every nonzero global linear limit satisfying the cap and mild formulation. Thus each nonzero cap limit has a finite dilated horizon.
- **claim-positive-history-lower-bound** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-br-positive-histories)): Let $0<a<b<\infty$. Suppose $f\ge0$ solves $D_tf=2P(\widetilde B,f)-\nu f$ in the local transport sense, both collision terms are locally integrable, and the chosen rate representatives $q_\sigma$ are bounded on compact positive-time phase-space sets. Start with any nonnegative bounded, integrable portion of $f(a)$. Integration of any measurable collection of bounded finite histories with weights <a href="#br:history-weight" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="br:history-weight">[br:history-weight]</a> gives a measure dominated by $f(b)$.
  - Explanation: Integrated positive finite collision histories give measures dominated by the terminal solution. Restricting histories or the initial portion preserves this lower bound.
- **claim-reference-retention-and-potential** (bound, [source](#stmt-br-retention)): For every $v\in E$, $p(v)\ge25/26$. The reference history sum restricted to velocities in $E$ is represented by a Markov flight with velocity jump rate $\lambda(v)=2p(v)\nu_0(v)$, jump law $J(v,\,\mathrm dv')\mathbf 1_E(v')/p(v)$, and multiplicative weight $$\exp\left(\int_0^A \mathcal W(V_s)\,\mathrm ds\right), \qquad \mathcal W(v)=(2p(v)-1)\nu_0(v).$$ One may take $$\label{br:potential-bounds} 0<c_0=\frac{48\pi}{13}\le \mathcal W(v),\qquad \lambda(v)\le C_0=48\pi\quad(v\in E).$$
  - Explanation: The bounded reference velocity region retains most children. Restricted branching histories equal a Markov flight weighted by a uniformly positive potential and having a bounded jump rate.
- **claim-reference-volume-minorization** (bound, [source](#stmt-br-minorization)): There are $r_0>0$ and $\varepsilon_0>0$ such that $$\label{br:doeblin} P_1(v,A)\ge\varepsilon_0\|A\cap B(0,r_0)\|\quad(v\in E)$$ for every Borel set $A\subset E$. For each $\zeta>0$, there is also $\varepsilon_\zeta>0$ such that $$P_1(v,B(e_1,\zeta)\cap\{\|v'\|<D\})\ge\varepsilon_\zeta\quad(v\in E).$$
  - Explanation: A finite collision construction gives a common positive volume component in the transition law and positive probability of reaching a transverse velocity neighborhood.
- **claim-reference-mixing-and-displacement** (bound, [source](#stmt-br-mixing)): The reference process has a stationary probability law $\pi$ with $\int v\,\mathrm d\pi(v)=0$. There are $C,c>0$ such that $$\sup_{v\in E}\\|P_t(v,\cdot)-\pi\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le Ce^{-ct}, \qquad \sup_{v\in E}\mathbb E_v\left\| A^{-1}\int_0^A V_s\,\mathrm ds\right\|^2\le\frac C A\quad(A\ge1).$$
  - Explanation: The reference velocity process has a symmetric stationary law, exponential uniform mixing, and a decaying second moment for its time-averaged velocity.
- **claim-reference-drift-block** (bound, [source](#stmt-br-reference-block)): There is a finite $L$ such that, for all $$A\in I_L:=\left[\frac L{1+d},\frac L{1-d}\right],\qquad v\in E,$$ reference branching histories with velocities in $E$, final velocity strictly inside $E$, and displacement $Z_A$ satisfying $$\label{br:strict-drift} \|Z_A-\varkappa Ae_1\|<\frac34d\varkappa A$$ have total weight greater than 8.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently long reference block combines bounded speeds, a prescribed transverse displacement, an interior terminal velocity, and a strict branching-weight gain.
- **claim-measurable-medium-robust-block** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-br-robust-block)): For these fixed $\varkappa,L$, there are $J_{\mathrm{br}}\in\mathbb N$ and $\varepsilon_*\in(0,1/4)$ with the following property. Suppose a measurable medium obeys, throughout every speed-$D$ excursion from a specified starting point over a duration $A\in I_L$, $$\label{br:medium-closeness} \|b_\sigma-1\|\le\varepsilon_*, \qquad \|w_\sigma-\sigma e_3\|\le\varepsilon_* \quad(\sigma=\pm1).$$ From each starting velocity in $E$, histories with between $1$ and $J_{\mathrm{br}}$ collisions, speed at most $D$ on every free flight, every postcollision velocity and the final velocity strictly inside $E$, and $$\label{br:robust-drift} \|Z_A-\varkappa Ae_1\|<d\varkappa A$$ have total weight greater than 3. The initial free flight may have speed $D$. No continuity of the medium is needed.
  - Explanation: Compact finite families of reference histories retain a strict growth and displacement margin under uniformly small measurable perturbations of the beam densities and velocities.
- **claim-moving-box-mass-growth** (bound, [source](#stmt-br-block-growth)): For the parameters just fixed, let $a_{\rm br}=L\varkappa/(\theta K)$. There is a time $t_{\mathrm{br}}>0$, independent of the mesh and $l\in[0,1]$, such that every density satisfying the equation, cap, and mild formula of Proposition <a href="#prop:finite-dilated-horizon" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:finite-dilated-horizon">44</a> on an interval containing $[t,(1+a_{\rm br})t]$, with $t\ge t_{\mathrm{br}}$, obeys, at its positive-time traces, $$\label{br:mass-growth} M((1+a_{\rm br})t)\ge3M(t),\qquad M(s):=\int_{\mathcal B_s}g(s,x,v)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv.$$
  - Explanation: With the selected parameters, positive-time mass in a moving box multiplies across a relative time step uniformly over meshes and lower endpoints.
- **claim-accessibility-of-late-moving-boxes** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-br-accessibility)): For the fixed parameters above, let $g\not\equiv0$ solve <a href="#eq:limit-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:limit-equation">[eq:limit-equation]</a> on $0<t<\infty$ with $0\le g\le(t+l)H$, for any allowed mesh and $l\in[0,1]$. Then $\int_{\mathcal B_t}g(t,x,v)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv>0$ at arbitrarily large finite times.
  - Explanation: Every nonzero global capped linear solution sends positive mass into moving boxes at arbitrarily late finite times, enabling the growth contradiction.
- **claim-dormant-dilation-vanishing** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sep-dormant-vanishing)): On the periodic lift centered at the origin, $$\frac{F^{\mathrm d}(Tt,Tx,v)}{T m_T}\longrightarrow0 \qquad(T\downarrow0)$$ in local $L^1$ on $$\mathcal V=(0,1)\times\mathbb R^3_x\times \{v\in\mathbb R^3:\bar v\ne0\}.$$
  - Explanation: The normalized dormant branch vanishes locally away from the velocity axis under the horizon dilation. Comparison with the nonzero cap dilation then separates the global solutions.

      "exact_statement": "There exist $R<\\infty$ and a nonnegative datum $F_0$ with $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}_vF_0\\subset B_R(0),\\qquad\n \\int_\\Omega F_0(1+|v|^2+|\\log F_0|)\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv<\\infty,$$ for which <a href=\"#eq:collision\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:collision\">[eq:collision]</a> has two global entropy solutions with local conservation in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:admissible\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:admissible\">1</a>, differing on a set of positive $(t,x,v)$-measure. Both solutions belong to $C([0,\\infty);L^1(\\Omega))$. Moreover, for every finite $T$ and every $k\\ge0$, they satisfy $$\\label{eq:strong-collision-integrability}\n \\int_0^T\\!\\!\\int_\\Omega\n \\langle v\\rangle^k\\bigl[Q^+(F,F)+Q^-(F,F)\\bigr]\n                       \\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm dt<\\infty,\n \\qquad \\langle v\\rangle=(1+|v|^2)^{1/2}.$$",
      "explanation": "A specially constructed datum admits distinct global entropy solutions with exact local conservation, strong time continuity, and separately integrable weighted collision gains and losses.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "eq:collision",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathbb T^3=\\mathbb R^3/\\mathbb Z^3$ have volume one and put $\\Omega=\\mathbb T^3_x\\times\\mathbb R^3_v$.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        },

          "exact_quote": "for every finite $T$ and every $k\\ge0",

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-carleman",
        "exact_quote": "For the normalization in <a href=\"#eq:collision\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:collision\">[eq:collision]</a>, the symmetric bilinear gain is $$\\label{ker:carleman}\n P(c,h)(v)\n =2\\int_{\\mathbb R^3}c(p)\\,\\,\\mathrm dp\n       \\int_{\\mathbb R^3}\n       \\delta\\bigl((v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)\\bigr)h(\\xi)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\xi.$$ For fixed $p\\ne v$, the inner delta denotes surface measure on the plane $(v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)=0$, divided by $|v-p|$. For incoming velocities $p,\\xi$, the outgoing measure has total mass $2\\pi|p-\\xi|$ and is uniform on the sphere with diameter $[p,\\xi]$. Formula <a href=\"#ker:carleman\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ker:carleman\">[ker:carleman]</a> also defines the gain when $c$ is a nonnegative velocity measure and $h$ is a nonnegative density, whenever the displayed integral is finite almost everywhere.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-carleman",
          "exact_quote": "For the normalization in <a href=\"#eq:collision\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:collision\">[eq:collision]</a>, the symmetric bilinear gain is $$\\label{ker:carleman}\n P(c,h)(v)\n =2\\int_{\\mathbb R^3}c(p)\\,\\,\\mathrm dp\n       \\int_{\\mathbb R^3}\n       \\delta\\bigl((v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)\\bigr)h(\\xi)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\xi.$$ For fixed $p\\ne v$, the inner delta denotes surface measure on the plane $(v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)=0$, divided by $|v-p|$. For incoming velocities $p,\\xi$, the outgoing measure has total mass $2\\pi|p-\\xi|$ and is uniform on the sphere with diameter $[p,\\xi]$.           "source_id": "PM-363.1"

      "exact_statement": "For the normalization in <a href=\"#eq:collision\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:collision\">[eq:collision]</a>, the symmetric bilinear gain is $$\\label{ker:carleman}\n P(c,h)(v)\n =2\\int_{\\mathbb R^3}c(p)\\,\\,\\mathrm dp\n       \\int_{\\mathbb R^3}\n       \\delta\\bigl((v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)\\bigr)h(\\xi)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\xi.$$ For fixed $p\\ne v$, the inner delta denotes surface measure on the plane $(v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)=0$, divided by $|v-p|$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ker-geometric-slice",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\lambda$ be a probability mixture of the measures $\\mathcal U_d$, $d>0$, and $\\delta_0$, and let $\\rho$ be any axial probability measure. No bound on its density or its support is required. Put $c=\\lambda\\otimes\\rho$. For $R\\geq1$, $\\bar v\\ne0$, and an interval $I\\subset\\mathbb R$ of length $0<\\ell\\leq2R$, one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\frac{1}{W(v)}\\int c(\\,\\mathrm dp)\n   \\int_{|\\xi-v|\\leq R}\n    \\delta\\bigl((v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)\\bigr)W(\\xi)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\xi\n     \\leq C_q R^2,\\label{ker:geometric-total}\\\\\n &\\frac{1}{W(v)}\\int c(\\,\\mathrm dp)\n   \\int_{\\substack{|\\xi-v|\\leq R\\\\ \\xi_3\\in I}}\n    \\delta\\bigl((v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)\\bigr)W(\\xi)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\xi\n     \\leq C_q\\bigl(R\\ell+R^{q-1}\\ell^{2-q}\\bigr).\n     \\label{ker:geometric-interval}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constants are independent of all transverse widths and of $\\rho$. In particular, these statements hold with $\\lambda=\\mathcal G_d$ for every $d\\geq0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ker-geometric-slice",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\lambda$ be a probability mixture of the measures $\\mathcal U_d$, $d>0$, and $\\delta_0$, and let $\\rho$ be any axial probability measure.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-sheet-bound",
        "exact_quote": "For every $L<\\infty$ there is $C_{q,m,L}<\\infty$ such that $$\\label{ker:sheet-gain}\n P(c,H)(v)\\leq C_{q,m,L}H(v)\n \\quad\\hbox{for almost every }v,\n \\qquad c\\in\\mathcal S_L.$$ More quantitatively, for $R\\geq1$, $$\\label{ker:shell-tail}\n \\mathcal K_c\\bigl(v;\n      R<|p|+|\\xi-v|\\leq2R\\bigr)\n \\leq C_{q,m,L}R^2e^{-cR^2},$$ where $c>0$ is an absolute constant. The same conclusions, multiplied by $A_0$, hold under sheet domination with amplitude $A_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-kernel-tightness",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\leq L<\\infty$. Uniformly for $c\\in\\mathcal S_L$ and outgoing velocities off the axis, the tails in $|p|+|\\xi-v|$ are negligible: $$\\label{ker:uniform-tail}\n \\lim_{R\\to\\infty}\\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_v\n \\mathcal K_c(v;|p|+|\\xi-v|>R)=0.$$ Moreover, $$\\label{ker:large-output}\n \\lim_{M\\to\\infty}\\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_{|v|>M}\\mathcal K_c(v;\\mathbb R^3\\times\\mathbb R^3)=0.$$ Both conclusions hold under uniformly bounded sheet amplitudes.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-kernel-tightness",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\leq L<\\infty$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ker-parameter-sheets",
        "exact_quote": "Let a measure in parameters $w=(\\bar w,w_3)\\in\\mathbb R^2\\times\\mathbb R$ have density $j$ satisfying $$0\\leq j(w)\\leq C_0\\pi^{-3/2}e^{-|w|^2}.$$ Let its velocity image be given by $$\\label{ker:parameter-map}\n \\bar p=d\\bar w,\\qquad p_3=\\Phi(w_3),\\qquad\n 0\\leq d\\leq\\tfrac1{16},\\qquad\n |\\Phi(a)|\\leq A+\\delta|a|,\\quad0\\leq\\delta\\leq\\tfrac1{16},$$ where $\\Phi$ is merely measurable. The image measure is dominated by $C_0$ times a sheet in $\\mathcal S_{L_A}$, with $L_A$ depending only on $A$. Its gain satisfies Lemma <a href=\"#lem:sheet-bound\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:sheet-bound\">6</a> with a constant depending only on $q,m,A,C_0$.*\n\n\n*If $c_{>B}$ is the image of the restricted parameter measure $\\mathbf 1_{|w|>B}j(w)\\,\\,\\mathrm dw$, then $$\\label{ker:parameter-tail}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_v\\frac{P(c_{>B},H)(v)}{H(v)}\n \\leq C_{q,m,A,C_0}e^{-B^2/2},\\qquad B\\geq0.$$ Thus parameter truncations are valid in the normalized collision measure, even when $\\Phi$ compresses intervals or has atoms in its image law. Probability mixtures of measurable families with common $q,m,A,C_0$ and the width bounds in <a href=\"#ker:parameter-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ker:parameter-map\">[ker:parameter-map]</a> preserve these assertions. More generally, a positive mixing measure of total mass $B_0<\\infty$ multiplies the domination amplitude and quantitative upper bounds by $B_0$; the sheet parameter $L_A$ is unchanged. For <a href=\"#ker:parameter-tail\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ker:parameter-tail\">[ker:parameter-tail]</a>, impose the restriction $|w|>B$ in each component before mixing. Additional measurable masks taking values in $[0,1]$ preserve the assertions.",

      },

The image measure is dominated by $C_0$ times a sheet in $\\mathcal S_{L_A}$, with $L_A$ depending only on $A$. Its gain satisfies Lemma <a href=\"#lem:sheet-bound\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:sheet-bound\">6</a> with a constant depending only on $q,m,A,C_0$.*\n\n\n*If $c_{>B}$ is the image of the restricted parameter measure $\\mathbf 1_{|w|>B}j(w)\\,\\,\\mathrm dw$, then $$\\label{ker:parameter-tail}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_v\\frac{P(c_{>B},H)(v)}{H(v)}\n \\leq C_{q,m,A,C_0}e^{-B^2/2},\\qquad B\\geq0.$$ Thus parameter truncations are valid in the normalized collision measure, even when $\\Phi$ compresses intervals or has atoms in its image law. Probability mixtures of measurable families with common $q,m,A,C_0$ and the width bounds in <a href=\"#ker:parameter-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ker:parameter-map\">[ker:parameter-map]</a> preserve these assertions.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-con-datum",
        "exact_quote": "The density $F_0$ is nonnegative, has bounded velocity support, and satisfies $$\\int F_0(1+|v|^2+|\\log F_0|)\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv<\\infty.$$ Its truncations with $j\\le N$, with or without a seed <a href=\"#con:seed\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"con:seed\">[con:seed]</a>, converge to $F_0$ in weighted $L^1$ for every fixed polynomial velocity weight and in relative entropy with respect to either $M$ or $\\mu$. The convergence is uniform for $0<T\\le t_1$ and $0\\le\\lambda\\le L_0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-con-finite-evolution",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed $N,b,T,\\lambda$, the colored equations have a unique nonnegative local mild solution with bounded total $H$-norm. It continues while that norm remains bounded, with the prescribed transfers at retirement times, and depends continuously in this norm on its initial data on every common bounded interval of existence. The norm of each component is continuous between transfers.*\n\n\n*On every such interval, total mass, momentum, and energy are conserved. The exact local collision-invariant balances hold, with all polynomial velocity fluxes integrable. For $\\rho=M$ or $\\rho=\\mu$, one has at every time of existence $$\\mathcal H_\\rho(F(t))+\n       \\int_0^t\\mathcal D^b(F(s))\\,\\mathrm ds\n       \\le\\mathcal H_\\rho(F(0)),\n \\label{con:finite-entropy}$$ where $\\mathcal D^b$ is the entropy dissipation with the same cutoff.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "At a fixed $(t,x)$, the velocity measure $f_i(t,x,p)\\,\\mathrm dp$ is the pushforward of $$\\frac{K S(\\gamma(z_c-tp_3)/s)}\n          {s+t\\alpha\\gamma S(\\gamma(z_c-tp_3)/s)}\\,J(w)\\,\\mathrm dw,\\qquad\n \\bar p=d_s\\bar w,\\quad\n p_3=a_s^\\sigma(z_c-tp_3)+d_sw_3,\n \\label{cold:core-formula}$$ with the initial spatial masks evaluated at $x-tp$. For $t\\le L_s$, it is dominated by probability sheets with total amplitude $C/(t+s)$. The remainder $\\epsilon_s m_i\\,\\mathrm dp$ has sheet amplitude at most $$C L_sd_s/s^2\\le Cs^{15/2}\\le C/(t+s).\n \\label{cold:remainder-amplitude}$$ Thus the full upper bound $\\mathcal U_i\\,\\mathrm dp$ has sheet amplitude $C/(t+s)$. In particular, for every fixed $k\\ge0$, a main color satisfies $$\\int\\langle p\\rangle^k c_i(t,x,p)\\,\\mathrm dp\\le\\frac{C_k}{t+s},\\qquad\n \\int_{|p|>R}\\langle p\\rangle^k c_i(t,x,p)\\,\\mathrm dp\n       \\le\\frac{C_k e^{-c_kR^2}}{t+s}\\quad(R\\ge3).\n \\label{cold:moments}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-early-bath",
        "exact_quote": "There are $t_1\\in(0,1/32]$, $\\beta'>4$, and constants $C,c>0$, independent of $\\delta$, $R_\\mu$, and $s_0$, such that every finite evolution of <a href=\"#con:colors\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"con:colors\">[con:colors]</a> satisfies, on its interval of existence, $$\\label{early:bath-bound}\n 0\\le u(t,x,v)\\le C e^{-\\beta'|v|^2}\n       \\exp\\!\\left(-\\frac{cD_0(x)^2}{t}\\right),\n \\qquad 0<t\\le t_1,\n \\qquad D_0(x)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(x,D_{\\rm hole}^c).$$ For sufficiently small $s_0$ depending on $\\delta$, one also has $$\\label{early:hole-distance}\n D_0(x)\\ge\\delta\n \\qquad (0\\le t\\le t_1,\\ x\\in\\mathcal O(t)).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-early-cap-selection",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed $\\delta>0$, there are $\\bar s_0,T_0>0$, uniform in $N$ and the collision cutoff, with the following property. For each $0<s_0\\le\\bar s_0$, each $0<T\\le T_0$, and each finite approximation, a seed parameter $\\lambda\\in(0,L_0)$ gives a solution on $[0,T]$ satisfying $$\\label{early:cap}\n Z(t)\\le m_T(t+s_N)\\quad(0\\le t\\le T),\n \\qquad\n \\max_{0\\le t\\le T}\\frac{Z(t)}{m_T(t+s_N)}=1.$$ The dormant solution exists on a fixed initial interval and satisfies $$\\label{early:dormant-bound}\n Z(t)\\le C'_{A,\\delta}t^A.$$ The interval and constant in <a href=\"#early:dormant-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"early:dormant-bound\">[early:dormant-bound]</a> are independent of $N$, the collision cutoff, and sufficiently small $s_0$.",

        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\delta>0$ and $\\zeta\\in(0,1)$.         "exact_quote": "There is a constant $\\kappa>0$, depending only on the fixed Maxwellian $\\mu$ and $t_1$, with the following property.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-global-control",
        "exact_quote": "The construction parameters can be fixed so that every dormant finite evolution and every cap solution with $0<T\\le T_0$ continues globally. There are constants $B<\\infty$ and $\\kappa>0$, independent of $N$, $b_N\\ge1$, and $T$, such that $$\\label{global:uniform-bound}\n \\|w(t)\\|_H\\le B,\\qquad \\nu_F^{b_N}(t,x,v)\\ge\\kappa,\n \\qquad t\\ge t_1.$$ Every active jet satisfies <a href=\"#global:cold-decay\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"global:cold-decay\">[global:cold-decay]</a> through its retirement, the total retirement increment is bounded by <a href=\"#global:retirement\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"global:retirement\">[global:retirement]</a>, and the hot component satisfies the small bound of <a href=\"#early:continuation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"early:continuation\">24</a> on $[0,t_1]$.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-global-control",
          "exact_quote": "The construction parameters can be fixed so that every dormant finite evolution and every cap solution with $0<T\\le T_0$ continues globally. There are constants $B<\\infty$ and $\\kappa>0$, independent of $N$, $b_N\\ge1$, and $T$, such that $$\\label{global:uniform-bound}\n \\|w(t)\\|_H\\le B,\\qquad \\nu_F^{b_N}(t,x,v)\\ge\\kappa,\n \\qquad t\\ge t_1.$$ Every active jet satisfies <a href=\"#global:cold-decay\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"global:cold-decay\">[global:cold-decay]</a> through its retirement, the total retirement increment is bounded by <a href=\"#global:retirement\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"global:retirement\">[global:retirement]</a>, and the hot component satisfies the small bound of <a href=\"#early:continuation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"early:continuation\">24</a> on $[0,t_1]$.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The construction parameters can be fixed so that every dormant finite evolution and every cap solution with $0<T\\le T_0$ continues globally.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-global-limits",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the construction parameters supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:global-control\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:global-control\">28</a>, and let $b_N\\to\\infty$. Either the dormant family, or a family of cap solutions at one fixed horizon $T\\in(0,T_0]$, has a subsequence of total densities $F_N$ converging weakly in $L^1([0,\\tau]\\times\\Omega)$ for every $\\tau<\\infty$ to a nonnegative density $F$. This subsequence can be chosen so that $F_N(t)\\rightharpoonup F(t)$ in $L^1(\\Omega)$ at every $t\\ge0$, with uniformly small polynomially weighted velocity tails on each bounded time interval.*\n\n\n*The limit has initial datum $F_0$ and is a global entropy solution obeying local conservation in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:admissible\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:admissible\">1</a>. More precisely, $$\\label{comp:strong-properties}\n F\\in C([0,\\infty);L^1(\\Omega)),\\qquad\n \\int_0^\\tau\\!\\int_\\Omega\n \\langle v\\rangle^k\\bigl(F+Q^+(F,F)+Q^-(F,F)\\bigr)\n \\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm dt<\\infty\n \\quad(k\\ge0,\\ \\tau<\\infty).$$ Total mass, momentum, and energy are conserved at every time. The common early-time pointwise majorant of the dormant approximations also bounds any dormant limit.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-global-limits",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the construction parameters supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:global-control\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:global-control\">28</a>, and let $b_N\\to\\infty$. This subsequence can be chosen so that $F_N(t)\\rightharpoonup F(t)$ in $L^1(\\Omega)$ at every $t\\ge0$, with uniformly small polynomially weighted velocity tails on each bounded time interval.*\n\n\n*The limit has initial datum $F_0$ and is a global entropy solution obeying local conservation in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:admissible\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:admissible\">1</a>. More precisely, $$\\label{comp:strong-properties}\n F\\in C([0,\\infty);L^1(\\Omega)),\\qquad\n \\int_0^\\tau\\!\\int_\\Omega\n \\langle v\\rangle^k\\bigl(F+Q^+(F,F)+Q^-(F,F)\\bigr)\n \\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm dt<\\infty\n \\quad(k\\ge0,\\ \\tau<\\infty).$$ Total mass, momentum, and energy are conserved at every time. The common early-time pointwise majorant of the dormant approximations also bounds any dormant limit.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix the construction parameters supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:global-control\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:global-control\">28</a>, and let $b_N\\to\\infty$. Either the dormant family, or a family of cap solutions at one fixed horizon $T\\in(0,T_0]$, has a subsequence of total densities $F_N$ converging weakly in $L^1([0,\\tau]\\times\\Omega)$ for every $\\tau<\\infty$ to a nonnegative density $F$. This subsequence can be chosen so that $F_N(t)\\rightharpoonup F(t)$ in $L^1(\\Omega)$ at every $t\\ge0$, with uniformly small polynomially weighted velocity tails on each bounded time interval.*\n\n\n*The limit has initial datum $F_0$ and is a global entropy solution obeying local conservation in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:admissible\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:admissible\">1</a>. More precisely, $$\\label{comp:strong-properties}\n F\\in C([0,\\infty);L^1(\\Omega)),\\qquad\n \\int_0^\\tau\\!\\int_\\Omega\n \\langle v\\rangle^k\\bigl(F+Q^+(F,F)+Q^-(F,F)\\bigr)\n \\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm dt<\\infty\n \\quad(k\\ge0,\\ \\tau<\\infty).$$ Total mass, momentum, and energy are conserved at every time. The common early-time pointwise majorant of the dormant approximations also bounds any dormant limit.",
      "explanation": "Removing jet truncation and collision cutoff produces global entropy limits with strong time traces and exact local balances. Separate weighted collision integrability prevents conservation defects.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-comp-averaging",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f_n,g_n$ be locally uniformly integrable in $L^1$ on an open region of time, space, and velocity, and suppose that $(\\partial_t+v\\cdot\\nabla_x)f_n=g_n$ there in distributions. For every smooth compactly supported velocity test $\\psi$, the averages $\\int\\psi(v)f_n(t,x,v)\\,\\mathrm dv$ are relatively compact in local $L^1_{t,x}$ wherever the hypotheses hold for all velocities in a neighborhood of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\psi$. The spatial domain may be a flat torus or an open subset of $\\mathbb R^3$.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-blowup-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Every sequence <a href=\"#eq:rescaling\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rescaling\">[eq:rescaling]</a> has a subsequence for which $h_{r_N}\\rightharpoonup g$ in local spacetime $L^1$ on $(0,U)\\times\\mathbb R^3_x\\times\\mathbb R^3_v$, where $$\\label{eq:limit-equation}\n0\\leq g(t,x,v)\\leq(t+l)H(v),\\qquad\nD_tg=2P(B,g)-g\\nu_B.$$ Here $B$ is <a href=\"#eq:background\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:background\">[eq:background]</a>; its gain and loss are defined by integration against the velocity measure. Both terms on the right are locally integrable, with a velocity majorant uniform in position on each compact positive-time interval. Compactly supported velocity averages of $h_{r_N}$ converge strongly in local $L^1_{t,x}$. The limit has a locally strongly continuous $L^1$ representative at positive times and obeys the integrating-factor formula <a href=\"#dil:mild\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dil:mild\">[dil:mild]</a> below.*\n\n\n*If $l>0$, pass also to a limit $\\lambda_N\\to\\lambda_0$ of the seed parameters. The conclusion and its local continuity extend to time zero, with $$\\label{dil:initial-trace}\ng(0,x,v)=\\lambda_0 l\\,\\chi(x/l,v).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-dil-free-core-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $N$, define the velocity measure $$A_N(t,x,\\,\\mathrm dp)\n   =r_N\\sum_{i:\\,j\\le N}f_i(r_Nt,r_Nx,p)\\,\\mathrm dp,\n       \\qquad 0\\le t\\le1.$$ This definition uses free transport of the initial colors and is meaningful even if $t>U_N$. At each position, at most one summand is nonzero. Its velocity moments and normalized gain obey $$\\int\\langle p\\rangle^k A_N(t,x,\\,\\mathrm dp)\n      \\le\\frac{C_k}{t+l_N},\\qquad\n P(A_N,H)\\le\\frac{C}{t+l_N}H.$$ There are numbers $\\varepsilon_N\\downarrow0$ such that, on $0\\le t\\le\\min(1,U_N)$, the hot component obeys the pathwise upper comparison $$\\label{dil:positive-comparison}\n D_th_{r_N}\\le2P(A_N,h_{r_N})+\\varepsilon_NH.$$ The bounds and comparison hold on full-measure endpoint sets at each time section, with representatives given by their path formulas.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-nonzero-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Consider any sequence of cap solutions with horizons $T_n\\to0$, approximation indices $N_n\\to\\infty$, and $s_{N_n}/T_n\\to0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nc-core-kernels",
        "exact_quote": "There are nonnegative velocity measures $A_n(t,x,\\,\\mathrm dp)$ and numbers $\\varepsilon_n\\to0$ with the following properties. After discarding finitely many terms, the measures $A_n$ are defined from free transport on the whole interval $0\\le t\\le1$, independently of the cap interval $[0,U_n]$. The bounds on $A_n$ below hold on that whole interval; comparison with $h_n$ is used only while the cap bound holds.*\n\n\n1.  *On $0<t<\\min(1,U_n)$, along almost every free characteristic, $$\\label{nc:positive-equation}\n     D_th_n\\le 2P(A_n,h_n)+\\varepsilon_n H .$$ At each point $A_n$ contains at most one main free core. Its dilated scale $\\varrho=s/r_n$ satisfies $|y|\\asymp\\varrho$, with coordinates measured from its periodic copy, and $$\\frac{2P(A_n,H)}{H}\\le\\frac{C}{t+\\ell_n}.$$*\n\n\n2.  *Before spatial masks are imposed, a core of scale $\\varrho=s/r_n$ and sign $\\sigma$ is the pushforward of $$\\label{nc:core-parameters}\n     \\frac{K S\\bigl(\\gamma(z-tp_3)/\\varrho\\bigr)}\n          {\\varrho+t\\alpha\\gamma S\\bigl(\\gamma(z-tp_3)/\\varrho\\bigr)}\n     J(w)\\,\\mathrm dw ,\n     \\qquad\n     \\bar p=d_s\\bar w,\\quad\n     p_3=\\sigma+\\alpha\\tanh\\bigl(\\gamma(z-tp_3)/\\varrho\\bigr)+d_sw_3 .$$ The actual masks are measurable factors between zero and one. The density in <a href=\"#nc:core-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"nc:core-parameters\">[nc:core-parameters]</a> is at most $C(t+\\varrho)^{-1}J(w)$.*\n\n\n3.  *For $t\\ge a>0$, the normalized gain measure $$\\label{nc:normalized-kernel}\n     \\mathcal K_n(t,x,v;\\,\\mathrm dp\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\xi)\n     =\n     4A_n(t,x,\\,\\mathrm dp)\\,\n     \\delta\\bigl((v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)\\bigr)\n     \\frac{H(\\xi)}{H(v)}\\,\\mathrm d\\xi$$ has total mass at most $C/a$. Uniformly in its base point, the contributions from large $|p|+|\\xi-v|$, or from large retained Gaussian parameters in a product-sheet majorant, tend to zero. Its total mass tends uniformly to zero as $|v|\\to\\infty$.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Its dilated scale $\\varrho=s/r_n$ satisfies $|y|\\asymp\\varrho$, with coordinates measured from its periodic copy, and $$\\frac{2P(A_n,H)}{H}\\le\\frac{C}{t+\\ell_n}.$$*\n\n\n2.  *Before spatial masks are imposed, a core of scale $\\varrho=s/r_n$ and sign $\\sigma$ is the pushforward of $$\\label{nc:core-parameters}\n     \\frac{K S\\bigl(\\gamma(z-tp_3)/\\varrho\\bigr)}\n          {\\varrho+t\\alpha\\gamma S\\bigl(\\gamma(z-tp_3)/\\varrho\\bigr)}\n     J(w)\\,\\mathrm dw ,\n     \\qquad\n     \\bar p=d_s\\bar w,\\quad\n     p_3=\\sigma+\\alpha\\tanh\\bigl(\\gamma(z-tp_3)/\\varrho\\bigr)+d_sw_3 .$$ The actual masks are measurable factors between zero and one.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nc-endpoint-tightness",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $a>0$, and let $t_n\\in[a,1]$. In a five-fold integral $(\\mathcal V_n^5F_n)(t_n,x_n,v_n)$, assume $0\\le F_n\\le2$. For every prescribed error, one may restrict all $|p_i|+|v_i-v_{i-1}|$ and sheet parameters to fixed bounded sets at that error, uniformly in $n,t_n,x_n,v_n$. Moreover:*\n\n1.  *if $|v_n|\\to\\infty$, the whole integral tends to zero;*\n\n2.  *after retaining bounded endpoint velocities, an endpoint escaping every bounded neighborhood of every dilated periodic origin also has a vanishing integral.*\n\n*Consequently any sequence along which the five-fold integral has a positive lower bound has, after periodic translations and subselection, convergent endpoint positions and velocities.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-axis-kernel-estimates",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\leq L<\\infty$. The following conclusions hold uniformly for $c\\in\\mathcal S_L$ and for outgoing velocities off the axis.*\n\n\n\n1.  *For every fixed $M,\\delta>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n     &\\lim_{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0}\n     \\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_{\\substack{|v|\\leq M\\\\0<|\\bar v|\\leq\\varepsilon}}\n     \\mathcal K_c(v;|\\bar\\xi|\\geq\\delta)=0,\n     \\label{ker:axis-input}\\\\\n     &\\lim_{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0}\n     \\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_{\\substack{|v|\\leq M\\\\0<|\\bar v|\\leq\\varepsilon}}\n     \\mathcal K_c(v;|v_3-p_3|\\geq\\delta)=0.\n     \\label{ker:axis-mismatch}\n    \\end{aligned}$$*\n\n\n\n2.  *The axial input marginals are uniformly non-atomic. More precisely, for each $R\\geq1$ and each interval $I$ of length $0<\\ell\\leq1$, $$\\label{ker:axial-interval}\n     \\mathcal K_c(v;|p|+|\\xi-v|\\leq R,\\ \\xi_3\\in I)\n     \\leq C_{q,m,L,R}\\bigl(\\ell+\\ell^{2-q}\\bigr).$$ In combination with <a href=\"#ker:uniform-tail\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ker:uniform-tail\">[ker:uniform-tail]</a>, this gives $$\\label{ker:axial-modulus}\n     \\lim_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_v\n     \\sup_{|I|\\leq\\ell}\\mathcal K_c(v;\\xi_3\\in I)=0.$$*\n\n\n3.  *Let $\\mathcal S_L(\\varepsilon)$ denote the subclass whose elementary transverse widths are at most $\\varepsilon$. For every $M<\\infty$ and $r_0>0$, $$\\label{ker:off-axis-small-input}\n     \\lim_{\\delta\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0}\n     \\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L(\\varepsilon)}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_{\\substack{|v|\\leq M\\\\|\\bar v|\\geq r_0}}\n     \\mathcal K_c(v;|\\bar\\xi|<\\delta)=0.$$*\n\n*All these conclusions hold under uniformly bounded sheet amplitudes.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-nc-plane-parameters",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ne0$, $e=n/|n|$, and let $\\zeta$ be a bounded nonnegative function supported in $[-1,1]$ with integral one. For every nonnegative measurable $F$, $$\\label{nc:plane-identity}\n \\int_{n^\\perp} F(k)\\frac{\\,\\mathrm dA(k)}{|n|}\n =\n \\int_{\\mathbb R^3}F(n\\times L)|n|\\zeta(L\\cdot e)\\,\\mathrm dL .$$ When $\\delta\\le|n|\\le R$ and the plane integral is restricted to $|k|\\le R_1$, the corresponding parameter measure $\\mathbf 1_{\\{|n\\times L|\\le R_1\\}}|n|\\zeta(L\\cdot e)\\,\\mathrm dL$ is supported in $$|L|\\le\\sqrt{(R_1/\\delta)^2+1}$$ and has density at most $R\\|\\zeta\\|_\\infty$. This bound concerns the parameter measure before multiplication by $F(n\\times L)$. The identity can be applied successively when each normal is a measurable function of parameters chosen at earlier gains.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

This bound concerns the parameter measure before multiplication by $F(n\\times L)$.
