      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nc-uniform-pullback",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal O\\subset\\mathbb R^k$ be open, $k\\ge d$, and suppose $\\Phi_n,\\Phi:\\mathcal O\\to\\mathbb R^d$ satisfy $\\Phi_n\\to\\Phi$ in $C^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ and $\\operatorname{rank}D\\Phi=d$ almost everywhere. Let $K\\Subset\\mathcal O$ and $B\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be compact. Let $a_n$ be measurable, supported in $K$, with $|a_n|\\le A$. Let $F_n$ be measurable, supported in $B$, with $|F_n|\\le M$, and converging to zero in measure. Then $$\\label{nc:pullback-conclusion}\n \\int_{\\mathcal O}|F_n(\\Phi_n(q))a_n(q)|\\,\\mathrm dq\\longrightarrow0 .$$ No convergence or continuity of the densities $a_n$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-363.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nc-uniform-pullback",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal O\\subset\\mathbb R^k$ be open, $k\\ge d$, and suppose $\\Phi_n,\\Phi:\\mathcal O\\to\\mathbb R^d$ satisfy $\\Phi_n\\to\\Phi$ in $C^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ and $\\operatorname{rank}D\\Phi=d$ almost everywhere. Let $K\\Subset\\mathcal O$ and $B\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be compact. Let $a_n$ be measurable, supported in $K$, with $|a_n|\\le A$. Let $F_n$ be measurable, supported in $B$, with $|F_n|\\le M$, and converging to zero in measure. Then $$\\label{nc:pullback-conclusion}\n \\int_{\\mathcal O}|F_n(\\Phi_n(q))a_n(q)|\\,\\mathrm dq\\longrightarrow0 .$$ No convergence or continuity of the densities $a_n$ is required.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-five-gain-rank",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a nonaxial endpoint velocity $v_0$, an endpoint $(t_0,x_0)$ with $t_0>0$, five positive scales $\\varrho_i$, and five signs $\\sigma_i$. Given positive times $t_i$ and $L_i\\in\\mathbb R^3$, define recursively $$\\label{nc:rank-map}\n \\begin{split}\n x_i&=x_{i-1}-(t_{i-1}-t_i)v_{i-1},\\\\\n p_i&=u_{\\varrho_i,\\sigma_i}(t_i,z_i)e_3,\\qquad\n n_i=v_{i-1}-p_i,\\\\\n k_i&=n_i\\times L_i,\\qquad v_i=v_{i-1}+k_i ,\n \\qquad i=1,\\ldots,5 .\n \\end{split}$$ The map $$\\Phi:(t_1,\\ldots,t_5,L_1,\\ldots,L_5)\n       \\longmapsto(t_5,x_5,v_5)$$ is real analytic on the connected open set $(0,\\infty)^5\\times\\mathbb R^{15}$, and has rank seven almost everywhere. In particular it is a submersion almost everywhere on the ordered region $0<t_5<\\cdots<t_1<t_0$.*\n\n*For convergent endpoint sequences with a nonaxial limiting velocity, convergent positive scale sequences, and the finite-temperature core formulas <a href=\"#nc:core-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"nc:core-parameters\">[nc:core-parameters]</a>, the analogous maps converge in $C^1$ on compact parameter sets to $\\Phi$. Bounded measurable parameter densities supported in a common compact set therefore satisfy Lemma <a href=\"#nc:uniform-pullback\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"nc:uniform-pullback\">41</a>, including after any measurable spatial, velocity, or time restrictions.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-dilated-horizon",
        "exact_quote": "There are fixed choices $$K\\ge1,\\qquad 0<\\eta\\le\\log2,\\qquad\n0<\\alpha\\le\\tfrac14,\\qquad 0<\\gamma\\le1$$ with the following property. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Entropy solution with local conservation** ([source](#stmt-def-admissible)): Let $F_0\ge0$ have finite mass, energy, and absolute entropy. A global entropy solution with local conservation is a nonnegative function $F$ satisfying the following conditions. 1. $F\in C_w([0,\infty);L^1(\Omega))$, $F(0)=F_0$, with continuity against every bounded measurable test. For every finite $T$, $$\sup_{0\le t\le T}\int_\Omega F(t)(1+\|v\|^2+\|\log F(t)\|)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv<\infty.$$ 2. The two functions $Q^\pm(F,F)/(1+G)$ belong to $L^1([0,T]\times\Omega)$ for every finite $T$. For every $\beta\in C^1([0,\infty))$ with $\beta(0)=0$ and $\|\beta'(z)\|\le C_\beta/(1+z)$, and every smooth $\varphi$ periodic in $x$ and compactly supported in $[0,\infty)\times\mathbb R^3_v$, $$\label{eq:renormalized} \begin{split} &\int_0^\infty\!\!\int_\Omega \left[M\beta(G)D_t\varphi+\beta'(G)Q(F,F)\varphi\right] \,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv\,\mathrm dt\\ &\hspace{15mm} +\int_\Omega M\beta(F_0/M)\varphi(0,x,v)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv=0. \end{split}$$ 3. Total momentum is conserved. At every $t\ge0$, $$\int_\Omega \|v\|^2F(t)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv\le\int_\Omega \|v\|^2F_0\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv, \qquad \mathcal H_M(F(t))+\int_0^t\mathcal D(F(s))\,\mathrm ds \le\mathcal H_M(F_0).$$ 4. For every finite $T$, $$\int_0^T\!\!\int_\Omega (1+\|v\|^3)F\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv\,\mathrm dt<\infty.$$ For every $\psi\in\{1,v_1,v_2,v_3,\|v\|^2/2\}$ and $\phi\in C_c^\infty([0,\infty)\times\mathbb T^3)$, $$\label{eq:local-balances} \int_0^\infty\!\!\int_\Omega F\psi D_t\phi\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv\,\mathrm dt +\int_\Omega F_0\psi\phi(0,x)\,\mathrm dx\,\mathrm dv=0.$$
- **Uniform sheet class** ([source](#stmt-ker-sheet-class)): Fix $L\geq1$. An elementary sheet in $\mathcal S_L$ is a product probability measure $$\label{ker:sheet-definition} c(\,\mathrm dp)=\mathcal G_d(\,\mathrm d\bar p)\rho(\,\mathrm dp_3),\qquad 0\leq d\leq\tfrac18,\qquad \int_{\mathbb R}e^{8a^2}\rho(\,\mathrm da)\leq L.$$ We also allow probability mixtures of elementary sheets. An upper bound by a sheet with amplitude $A_0$ means domination as measures by $A_0$ times such a mixture.
- **Singular velocity majorant and weighted supremum** ([source](#eq:weight)): Our velocity weight is $$\label{eq:weight} H(v)=e^{-\|v\|^2}\langle v\rangle^{-m}W(v), \qquad W(v)=1+\|\bar v\|^{-q},\qquad \\|f\\|_H=\mathop{\mathrm{ess\,sup}}_{x,v}\frac{\|f(x,v)\|}{H(v)}.$$
- **Normalized sheet collision kernel** ([source](#ker:normalized-kernel)): For such a sheet and $\bar v\ne0$, define its normalized kernel by $$\label{ker:normalized-kernel} \mathcal K_c(v;\,\mathrm dp\,\,\mathrm d\xi) =2\,c(\,\mathrm dp)\, \delta\bigl((v-p)\cdot(\xi-v)\bigr) \frac{H(\xi)}{H(v)}\,\,\mathrm d\xi.$$
- **Geometric jet scales and thermal widths** ([source](#con:scales)): $$s_j=s_0e^{-j\eta},\quad p_j=s_j^2,\quad c_\eta=\frac{e^\eta-1}{16},\quad d_s=d_0s^{10},\quad S(r)=\operatorname{sech}^2r, \qquad j\ge1, \label{con:scales}$$ where $0<d_0\le1$ is fixed sufficiently small.
- **Initial cold jet density** ([source](#con:jet-datum)): A jet label is $i=(j,\sigma,n)$, with $n=(\bar n,n_3)\in\Lambda$. For $s=s_j$ and $(y_c,z_c)=x-n$, define $$c_i^0(x,v)= \mathbf 1_{E_j^\sigma}(y_c)\mathbf 1_{(-a,a)}(z_c) \frac K{s}S(\gamma z_c/s)d_s^{-3} J\!\left(\frac{v-a_s^\sigma(z_c)e_3}{d_s}\right). \label{con:jet-datum}$$ All sums over labels include the lattice copies.
- **Free cold core** ([source](#cold:free-core)): The free transport and its majorant are $$f_i(t,x,v)=c_i^0(x-tv,v), \label{cold:free-core}$$
- **Dilated axial background** ([source](#eq:background)): On an open mesh annulus $\varrho<\|y\|<e^\eta\varrho$, define the nonnegative velocity measure $$\label{eq:background} \begin{split} B(t,x,\,\mathrm dp) &=\sum_{\sigma=\pm1}b_{\varrho,\sigma}(t,z) \delta_{u_{\varrho,\sigma}(t,z)e_3}(\,\mathrm dp),\\ b_{\varrho,\sigma}(t,z) &=\frac{\theta K S(\gamma(z-tu_{\varrho,\sigma})/\varrho)} {\varrho+t\alpha\gamma S(\gamma(z-tu_{\varrho,\sigma})/\varrho)}. \end{split}$$
- **Branching history weight** ([source](#br:history-weight)): Its weight relative to $\prod_i\,\mathrm d\tau_i\,\mathrm d\varsigma(n_i)$ is $$\label{br:history-weight} \exp\left(-\int_a^b\nu(s,X_s,V_s)\,\mathrm ds\right) \prod_{i=1}^j2q_{\sigma_i}(\tau_i,X_{\tau_i},V_{\tau_i-}).$$

          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "Let $F_0\\ge0$ have finite mass, energy, and absolute entropy. A global entropy solution with local conservation is a nonnegative function $F$ satisfying the following conditions.\n\n1.  $F\\in C_w([0,\\infty);L^1(\\Omega))$, $F(0)=F_0$, with continuity against every bounded measurable test. For every finite $T$, $$\\sup_{0\\le t\\le T}\\int_\\Omega\n     F(t)(1+|v|^2+|\\log F(t)|)\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv<\\infty.$$\n\n\n2.  The two functions $Q^\\pm(F,F)/(1+G)$ belong to $L^1([0,T]\\times\\Omega)$ for every finite $T$. For every $\\beta\\in C^1([0,\\infty))$ with $\\beta(0)=0$ and $|\\beta'(z)|\\le C_\\beta/(1+z)$, and every smooth $\\varphi$ periodic in $x$ and compactly supported in $[0,\\infty)\\times\\mathbb R^3_v$, $$\\label{eq:renormalized}\n    \\begin{split}\n     &\\int_0^\\infty\\!\\!\\int_\\Omega\n     \\left[M\\beta(G)D_t\\varphi+\\beta'(G)Q(F,F)\\varphi\\right]\n                      \\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm dt\\\\\n     &\\hspace{15mm}\n     +\\int_\\Omega M\\beta(F_0/M)\\varphi(0,x,v)\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv=0.\n    \\end{split}$$\n\n3.  Total momentum is conserved. At every $t\\ge0$, $$\\int_\\Omega |v|^2F(t)\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\le\\int_\\Omega |v|^2F_0\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv,\n     \\qquad\n     \\mathcal H_M(F(t))+\\int_0^t\\mathcal D(F(s))\\,\\mathrm ds\n          \\le\\mathcal H_M(F_0).$$\n\n\n4.  For every finite $T$, $$\\int_0^T\\!\\!\\int_\\Omega (1+|v|^3)F\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm dt<\\infty.$$ For every $\\psi\\in\\{1,v_1,v_2,v_3,|v|^2/2\\}$ and $\\phi\\in C_c^\\infty([0,\\infty)\\times\\mathbb T^3)$, $$\\label{eq:local-balances}\n     \\int_0^\\infty\\!\\!\\int_\\Omega F\\psi D_t\\phi\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm dt\n       +\\int_\\Omega F_0\\psi\\phi(0,x)\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv=0.$$",

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-transverse-singularity-upper | q | < | $2$ | The lower restriction on the exponent is also required for the plane integrals. |
| q-polynomial-weight-order | m | = | $100$ | Fixed throughout the construction. |
| q-hot-gain-polynomial-threshold | m | > | $5$ | A sufficient threshold for summability of the dyadic shell estimate. |
| q-jet-retirement-time | L_s | = | $s^{-1/2}$ | The scale-dependent deadline for transfer into the merged component. |
| q-cold-remainder-sheet-amplitude | remainder sheet amplitude | ≤ | $Cs^{15/2}$ | Uniform over finite approximations while the color remains active. |
| q-global-damping-lower-bound | \nu_F^b(t,x,v) | ≥ | $\kappa$ | The damping constant depends on the fixed Maxwellian and bath time, while the entropy tolerance depends on the bootstrap size. |
| q-global-quadratic-gain | P^b(w,w) | ≤ | $C_5H$ | Entropy, hot tolerance, and base scale must be sufficiently small depending on the bootstrap size. |
| q-volterra-iteration-bound | \\|\mathcal V_n^jF(t)\\|_\infty | ≤ | $\\|F\\|_\infty\frac{\bigl(C\log(t/a)\bigr)^j}{j!}$ | The estimate is uniform in the approximation and uses ordered collision times. |
| q-reference-retention-probability | p(v) | ≥ | $25/26$ | Retention in the fixed bounded reference velocity ball. |
| q-reference-potential-lower-constant | c_0 | = | $\frac{48\pi}{13}$ | A uniform lower bound throughout the reference velocity region. |
| q-reference-jump-rate-upper-constant | C_0 | = | $48\pi$ | A uniform upper bound on its jump rate in the reference region. |
| q-relative-growth-block-duration | a_{\rm br} | = | $\frac{L\varkappa}{\theta K}$ | Fixed after selecting the reference block and construction parameters. |
| q-growth-versus-cap-threshold | (1+a_{\rm br})^4 | < | $3$ | The jet amplitude is increased until box-volume growth and cap growth are dominated by the mass multiplication factor. |
| q-moving-box-mass-multiplication | M((1+a_{\rm br})t) | ≥ | $3M(t)$ | The solution interval must contain the full growth block; the starting time threshold is uniform over meshes. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-carleman-geometry** [claim](#stmt-lem-carleman) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-carleman): Push forward reflection measure onto a diameter sphere and apply coarea. -> The symmetric plane formula and its extension to cold velocity measures.
- **step-geometric-slices** [claim](#stmt-ker-geometric-slice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ker-geometric-slice): Integrate transverse lines and axial intervals, then average disk directions and Gaussian disk mixtures. -> Uniform total and axial-slice collision-plane bounds.
- **step-sheet-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-sheet-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sheet-bound): Use collision energy factorization, exponential tilting of sheet laws, and dyadic shell summation. -> A uniform sheet gain bound and Gaussian shell tails.
- **step-sheet-tightness** [claim](#stmt-lem-kernel-tightness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-tightness): Remove shell tails and exploit a large transverse or axial collision divisor. -> Input tightness and large-output decay.
- **step-parameter-sheets** [claim](#stmt-ker-parameter-sheets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ker-parameter-sheets): Push forward product Gaussian majorants and widen parameter Gaussians on tails without inverting the axial map. -> Compressed parameter laws, masks, and finite mixtures preserve uniform gain and tail estimates.
- **step-hot-quadratic-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-hot-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hot-bound): Use incoming symmetry, retain a large incoming speed, and sum polynomially decaying shells of disk mixtures. -> The quadratic gain closes in the weighted supremum norm.
- **step-small-mass-gain** [claim](#stmt-lem-gain-small-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gain-small-mass): Delete large speeds, transverse-axis neighborhoods, and a collision diagonal before estimating the remaining gain by velocity mass. -> Small mass makes the normalized mixed gain small.
- **step-lattice-separation** [claim](#stmt-con-lattice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-con-lattice): Use the nonzero integer numerator in the quadratic-irrational separation identity. -> A transverse lower bound for nonzero period vectors.
- **step-datum-bounds** [claim](#stmt-con-datum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-con-datum): Sum mass and entropy contributions over disjoint geometric-scale prototypes and control the seeded entropy increment. -> An admissible bounded-velocity datum and convergent finite approximations with small relative entropy.
- **step-finite-local-theory** [claim](#stmt-con-finite-evolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-con-finite-evolution): Apply positive Picard iteration in free coordinates, collision symmetries, and regularized entropy identities. -> Unique local colored solutions, continuation, conservation, and entropy inequalities.
- **step-disjoint-enlargements** [claim](#stmt-cold-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cold-geometry): Compare annular and stripe margins within a copy, and use irrational transverse separation between copies. -> At most one active main label at each position.
- **step-cold-barrier** [claim](#stmt-cold-barrier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cold-barrier): Differentiate the implicit monotone profile along flights, complete a distance square, and use Maxwellian detailed balance. -> Transport and self-gain barrier inequalities and support containment.
- **step-active-color-comparison** [claim](#stmt-cold-control) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cold-control): Construct a positive gain supersolution and apply weighted Gronwall comparison; incorporate damping when available. -> Uniform free-core-plus-remainder control of each active color.
- **step-core-amplitudes** [claim](#stmt-cold-core-amplitude) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cold-core-amplitude): Compute the implicit axial Jacobian and dominate the resulting parameter measure by sheets. -> Inverse time-plus-scale amplitudes, moments, and velocity tails.
- **step-nonmain-tail-sum** [claim](#stmt-cold-nonmain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cold-nonmain): Extract a strong spatial-distance exponential, sum Gaussian lattice shells, and absorb polynomial losses. -> Small Gaussian domination of all nonmain colors.
- **step-retirement-and-volume** [claim](#stmt-cold-retirement-size) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cold-retirement-size): Bound retirement lattice sums polynomially and sum active tube volumes over geometric scales. -> A polynomial retirement norm and small total active spatial volume.
- **step-bath-barrier** [claim](#stmt-early-bath) / [proof](#proof-stmt-early-bath): Build a distance-to-hole Maxwellian supersolution and compare along free paths. -> Gaussian bath control with exponentially suppressed hole penetration.
- **step-early-sources** [claim](#stmt-early-source-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-early-source-estimates): Separate the unique main color from nonmain tails and exploit the bath hole at main positions. -> Uniform cold-bath gain and arbitrary-power cross-source bounds.
- **step-cap-selection** [claim](#stmt-early-cap-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-early-cap-selection): Compare the dormant branch with a power supersolution and use the endpoint of an open set of seed parameters. -> A seed parameter whose hot evolution attains the cap and a dormant power bound.
- **step-early-path-smallness** [claim](#stmt-early-path-source) / [proof](#proof-stmt-early-path-source): Delete large and near-axis output velocities, then bound the time a transverse free flight spends in thin cylinders. -> Small integrated source along every relevant free flight in essential norm.
- **step-early-continuation** [claim](#stmt-early-continuation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-early-continuation): Combine an early power comparison with later path-source smallness and close a strict hot-norm bootstrap. -> Uniform continuation to the bath time with prescribed small hot norm.
- **step-global-flight-average** [claim](#stmt-global-flight-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-global-flight-average): Change velocities to spatial flight positions, count periodic cells, and truncate the integrable velocity weight. -> Uniform small weighted damped and early flight averages.
- **step-positive-frequency** [claim](#stmt-global-frequency) / [proof](#proof-stmt-global-frequency): Generate positive mass in separated velocity balls using gain lower bounds and entropy-error averaging along backward flights. -> A damping constant fixed independently of the bootstrap size.
- **step-small-excess-gain** [claim](#stmt-global-gain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-global-gain): Compare the merged component with the stationary Maxwellian, average its positive excess along damped flights, and apply the small-mass gain estimate. -> A nonlinear gain bound independent of the bootstrap size.
- **step-global-bootstrap** [claim](#stmt-prop-global-control) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-global-control): Use an integrable-coefficient Gronwall bound, choose parameters in dependency order, and restart strictly below the bootstrap threshold after retirements. -> Global continuation of both finite families.
- **step-velocity-averaging** [claim](#stmt-comp-averaging) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-averaging): Represent transport by a damped inverse, truncate uniformly integrable sources, and control high Fourier frequencies of velocity averages. -> Strong local compactness of compact velocity averages.
- **step-global-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-global-limits) / [proof](#sec:compactness): Discard small annular scales, identify pair-product limits using velocity averaging, and pass entropy and local balances with weighted collision integrability. -> Global entropy limits with exact local conservation and strong weighted time traces.
- **step-dilated-linear-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-blowup-limit) / [proof](#sec:dilation): Compute core Jacobians, average stripes, pair cold weak convergence with strong hot averages, and remove the rescaled error terms. -> A linear kinetic limit with a measure-valued axial medium and a positive-time mild formula.
- **step-free-core-comparison** [claim](#stmt-dil-free-core-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dil-free-core-comparison): Replace actual cold colors by free-core upper bounds and discard nonnegative losses. -> A positive free-core comparison valid up to the cap.
- **step-core-kernels** [claim](#stmt-nc-core-kernels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-core-kernels): Normalize the free-core gain and apply sheet and Gaussian parameter tail estimates before spatial masks. -> Positive normalized kernels with bounded slab masses and uniform tails.
- **step-endpoint-tightness** [claim](#stmt-nc-endpoint-tightness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-endpoint-tightness): Delete normalized tails, exploit large-output decay, and bound amplitudes for paths escaping every dilated period point. -> Compact endpoints and bounded paths after periodic translation.
- **step-axis-kernel-estimates** [claim](#stmt-lem-axis-kernel-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-axis-kernel-estimates): Use transverse line and axial delta integration, axial-slice moduli, and shrinking-width Gaussian tail deletion. -> Near-axis matching, axial nonatomicity, and off-axis small-input deletion.
- **step-characteristic-matching** [claim](#stmt-nc-characteristic-matching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-characteristic-matching): Differentiate the monotone profile and compare its characteristic root; separate endpoint speeds from the interior small-scale branch. -> Backward mismatch control and finite collapsed-speed approximation.
- **step-two-axial-gains** [claim](#stmt-nc-two-axial-gains) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-two-axial-gains): Remove short axial intervals around collapsed and finite-scale speeds, then combine first-gain transverse concentration with second-gain mismatch suppression. -> Vanishing of consecutive gains at axial limiting endpoints.
- **step-plane-parameterization** [claim](#stmt-nc-plane-parameters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-plane-parameters): Split the redundant parameter into normal and tangential components and compute the plane-area scaling. -> A conditional volume parameterization with controlled support and density.
- **step-uniform-pullback** [claim](#stmt-nc-uniform-pullback) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-uniform-pullback): Delete a small neighborhood of critical points and cover the regular compact set by uniformly invertible coordinate charts. -> Vanishing weighted pullback integrals for inputs vanishing in measure.
- **step-five-gain-rank** [claim](#stmt-lem-five-gain-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-five-gain-rank): Exhibit a nonzero analytic minor at equal collision times and use analyticity and implicit differentiation. -> Almost-everywhere full rank and robust finite-temperature pullback control.
- **step-five-nonaxial-gains** [claim](#stmt-nc-five-nonaxial-gains) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-five-nonaxial-gains): Delete near-axis velocities, small spatial radii, horizon strips, and time-boundary strips; apply uniform pullback to finitely many scale and sign tuples. -> Vanishing of the iterated gain contribution at nonaxial endpoints.
- **step-cap-nonvanishing** [claim](#stmt-prop-nonzero-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nc-five-nonaxial-gains-2): Use the initial trace for a positive lower mesh endpoint; otherwise iterate the positive comparison and exclude both axial and nonaxial disappearance at cap endpoints. -> Every cap limit is nonzero on its open dilated interval.
- **step-positive-histories** [claim](#stmt-br-positive-histories) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-positive-histories): Iterate the nonnegative integrating-factor gain formula and identify each finite collision history weight. -> Selected history measures are dominated by the solution trace.
- **step-reference-retention** [claim](#stmt-br-retention) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-retention): Compute the squared child-speed distribution and change the history survival factor to that of a retained Markov process. -> A retention bound, positive potential, and bounded jump rate.
- **step-reference-minorization** [claim](#stmt-br-minorization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-minorization): Select angular collision patches with a full-rank output map, force separated collision times, and prohibit additional jumps. -> A common positive volume component and access to transverse velocities.
- **step-reference-mixing** [claim](#stmt-br-mixing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-mixing): Contract total variation using a common transition component and integrate the resulting velocity correlations. -> A stationary mean-zero law, uniform exponential mixing, and a time-average second-moment bound.
- **step-reference-block** [claim](#stmt-br-reference-block) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-reference-block): Combine a mixing interval, steering toward a transverse velocity, and a final collision-free flight; compensate the survival cost by the positive potential. -> Strict reference growth with prescribed displacement and interior terminal velocity.
- **step-robust-block** [claim](#stmt-br-robust-block) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-robust-block): Restrict to compact finite-history tests with strict margins and use a finite cover of reference starts and durations. -> A bounded-collision growth block stable under measurable medium perturbations.
- **step-moving-box-growth** [claim](#stmt-br-block-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-block-growth): Scale each starting box point to a reference-duration block and integrate all admissible bounded histories against truncated solution mass. -> Uniform multiplication of moving-box mass at late times.
- **step-moving-box-access** [claim](#stmt-br-accessibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-accessibility): Transport a positive nonaxial portion outside the hole, steer it through positive collision patches, and retain a final long free flight. -> Positive mass in arbitrarily late moving boxes.
- **step-finite-horizon** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-dilated-horizon) / [proof](#proof-stmt-br-accessibility-2): Iterate moving-box mass growth and compare it with the spatial-volume and cap upper bound. -> No nonzero global capped linear solution and hence a finite horizon for each nonzero cap limit.
- **step-dormant-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-sep-dormant-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sep-dormant-vanishing): Normalize dormant hot bounds, exponentially small bath penetration, and off-axis cold Gaussian tails under horizon dilation. -> Local normalized dormant vanishing away from the velocity axis.
- **step-separate-global-solutions** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sep-dormant-vanishing-2): Assume common shrinking-horizon limits, choose a weak-convergence diagonal, compare comparable dilations, and contradict cap nonvanishing. -> Distinct global locally conservative entropy solutions with the same bounded-velocity datum.

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-carleman",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-carleman",
          "exact_quote": "For the normalization in <a href=\"#eq:collision\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:collision\">[eq:collision]</a>, the symmetric bilinear gain is $$\\label{ker:carleman}\n P(c,h)(v)\n =2\\int_{\\mathbb R^3}c(p)\\,\\,\\mathrm dp\n       \\int_{\\mathbb R^3}\n       \\delta\\bigl((v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)\\bigr)h(\\xi)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\xi.$$ For fixed $p\\ne v$, the inner delta denotes surface measure on the plane $(v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)=0$, divided by $|v-p|$. For incoming velocities $p,\\xi$, the outgoing measure has total mass $2\\pi|p-\\xi|$ and is uniform on the sphere with diameter $[p,\\xi]$. Formula <a href=\"#ker:carleman\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ker:carleman\">[ker:carleman]</a> also defines the gain when $c$ is a nonnegative velocity measure and $h$ is a nonnegative density, whenever the displayed integral is finite almost everywhere.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-carleman-geometry",
      "inputs": [
        "The hard-sphere collision normalization."
      ],
      "method": "Push forward reflection measure onto a diameter sphere and apply coarea.",
      "output": "The symmetric plane formula and its extension to cold velocity measures.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-carleman"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ker-geometric-slice",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-carleman-geometry"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ker-geometric-slice",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\lambda$ be a probability mixture of the measures $\\mathcal U_d$, $d>0$, and $\\delta_0$, and let $\\rho$ be any axial probability measure. No bound on its density or its support is required. Put $c=\\lambda\\otimes\\rho$. For $R\\geq1$, $\\bar v\\ne0$, and an interval $I\\subset\\mathbb R$ of length $0<\\ell\\leq2R$, one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\frac{1}{W(v)}\\int c(\\,\\mathrm dp)\n   \\int_{|\\xi-v|\\leq R}\n    \\delta\\bigl((v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)\\bigr)W(\\xi)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\xi\n     \\leq C_q R^2,\\label{ker:geometric-total}\\\\\n &\\frac{1}{W(v)}\\int c(\\,\\mathrm dp)\n   \\int_{\\substack{|\\xi-v|\\leq R\\\\ \\xi_3\\in I}}\n    \\delta\\bigl((v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)\\bigr)W(\\xi)\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\xi\n     \\leq C_q\\bigl(R\\ell+R^{q-1}\\ell^{2-q}\\bigr).\n     \\label{ker:geometric-interval}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constants are independent of all transverse widths and of $\\rho$. In particular, these statements hold with $\\lambda=\\mathcal G_d$ for every $d\\geq0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-geometric-slices",
      "inputs": [
        "The transverse singular weight.",
        "An arbitrary axial probability law."
      ],
      "method": "Integrate transverse lines and axial intervals, then average disk directions and Gaussian disk mixtures.",
      "output": "Uniform total and axial-slice collision-plane bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-ker-geometric-slice"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-sheet-bound",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-geometric-slices"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sheet-bound",
          "exact_quote": "For every $L<\\infty$ there is $C_{q,m,L}<\\infty$ such that $$\\label{ker:sheet-gain}\n P(c,H)(v)\\leq C_{q,m,L}H(v)\n \\quad\\hbox{for almost every }v,\n \\qquad c\\in\\mathcal S_L.$$ More quantitatively, for $R\\geq1$, $$\\label{ker:shell-tail}\n \\mathcal K_c\\bigl(v;\n      R<|p|+|\\xi-v|\\leq2R\\bigr)\n \\leq C_{q,m,L}R^2e^{-cR^2},$$ where $c>0$ is an absolute constant. The same conclusions, multiplied by $A_0$, hold under sheet domination with amplitude $A_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-sheet-bound",
      "inputs": [
        "The geometric slice estimates.",
        "The axial exponential moment."
      ],
      "method": "Use collision energy factorization, exponential tilting of sheet laws, and dyadic shell summation.",
      "output": "A uniform sheet gain bound and Gaussian shell tails.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-sheet-bound"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-kernel-tightness",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-sheet-bound"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-kernel-tightness",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\leq L<\\infty$. Uniformly for $c\\in\\mathcal S_L$ and outgoing velocities off the axis, the tails in $|p|+|\\xi-v|$ are negligible: $$\\label{ker:uniform-tail}\n \\lim_{R\\to\\infty}\\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_v\n \\mathcal K_c(v;|p|+|\\xi-v|>R)=0.$$ Moreover, $$\\label{ker:large-output}\n \\lim_{M\\to\\infty}\\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_{|v|>M}\\mathcal K_c(v;\\mathbb R^3\\times\\mathbb R^3)=0.$$ Both conclusions hold under uniformly bounded sheet amplitudes.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-sheet-tightness",
      "inputs": [
        "The sheet shell estimate."
      ],
      "method": "Remove shell tails and exploit a large transverse or axial collision divisor.",
      "output": "Input tightness and large-output decay.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-kernel-tightness"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ker-parameter-sheets",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-sheet-bound"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ker-parameter-sheets",
          "exact_quote": "Let a measure in parameters $w=(\\bar w,w_3)\\in\\mathbb R^2\\times\\mathbb R$ have density $j$ satisfying $$0\\leq j(w)\\leq C_0\\pi^{-3/2}e^{-|w|^2}.$$ Let its velocity image be given by $$\\label{ker:parameter-map}\n \\bar p=d\\bar w,\\qquad p_3=\\Phi(w_3),\\qquad\n 0\\leq d\\leq\\tfrac1{16},\\qquad\n |\\Phi(a)|\\leq A+\\delta|a|,\\quad0\\leq\\delta\\leq\\tfrac1{16},$$ where $\\Phi$ is merely measurable. The image measure is dominated by $C_0$ times a sheet in $\\mathcal S_{L_A}$, with $L_A$ depending only on $A$. Its gain satisfies Lemma <a href=\"#lem:sheet-bound\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:sheet-bound\">6</a> with a constant depending only on $q,m,A,C_0$.*\n\n\n*If $c_{>B}$ is the image of the restricted parameter measure $\\mathbf 1_{|w|>B}j(w)\\,\\,\\mathrm dw$, then $$\\label{ker:parameter-tail}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_v\\frac{P(c_{>B},H)(v)}{H(v)}\n \\leq C_{q,m,A,C_0}e^{-B^2/2},\\qquad B\\geq0.$$ Thus parameter truncations are valid in the normalized collision measure, even when $\\Phi$ compresses intervals or has atoms in its image law. Probability mixtures of measurable families with common $q,m,A,C_0$ and the width bounds in <a href=\"#ker:parameter-map\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ker:parameter-map\">[ker:parameter-map]</a> preserve these assertions. More generally, a positive mixing measure of total mass $B_0<\\infty$ multiplies the domination amplitude and quantitative upper bounds by $B_0$; the sheet parameter $L_A$ is unchanged. For <a href=\"#ker:parameter-tail\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ker:parameter-tail\">[ker:parameter-tail]</a>, impose the restriction $|w|>B$ in each component before mixing. Additional measurable masks taking values in $[0,1]$ preserve the assertions.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-parameter-sheets",
      "inputs": [
        "Measurable axial growth control.",
        "Sheet gain domination."
      ],
      "method": "Push forward product Gaussian majorants and widen parameter Gaussians on tails without inverting the axial map.",
      "output": "Compressed parameter laws, masks, and finite mixtures preserve uniform gain and tail estimates.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-ker-parameter-sheets"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-con-datum",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-con-datum",
          "exact_quote": "The density $F_0$ is nonnegative, has bounded velocity support, and satisfies $$\\int F_0(1+|v|^2+|\\log F_0|)\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv<\\infty.$$ Its truncations with $j\\le N$, with or without a seed <a href=\"#con:seed\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"con:seed\">[con:seed]</a>, converge to $F_0$ in weighted $L^1$ for every fixed polynomial velocity weight and in relative entropy with respect to either $M$ or $\\mu$. The convergence is uniform for $0<T\\le t_1$ and $0\\le\\lambda\\le L_0$. Moreover their initial $\\mu$-relative entropy can be made arbitrarily small, uniformly in $N,T,\\lambda$, by first decreasing $\\delta$ and increasing $R_\\mu$, then decreasing $s_0$.",

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-con-datum"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-con-finite-evolution",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-con-finite-evolution",
          "exact_quote": "For fixed $N,b,T,\\lambda$, the colored equations have a unique nonnegative local mild solution with bounded total $H$-norm. It continues while that norm remains bounded, with the prescribed transfers at retirement times, and depends continuously in this norm on its initial data on every common bounded interval of existence. The norm of each component is continuous between transfers.*\n\n\n*On every such interval, total mass, momentum, and energy are conserved. The exact local collision-invariant balances hold, with all polynomial velocity fluxes integrable. For $\\rho=M$ or $\\rho=\\mu$, one has at every time of existence $$\\mathcal H_\\rho(F(t))+\n       \\int_0^t\\mathcal D^b(F(s))\\,\\mathrm ds\n       \\le\\mathcal H_\\rho(F(0)),\n \\label{con:finite-entropy}$$ where $\\mathcal D^b$ is the entropy dissipation with the same cutoff.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }

For sufficiently small $s_0$, $f_i(t,x,v)>0$ implies $x\\in\\mathcal O_i(t)$ throughout the same time interval.",

          "anchor": "stmt-cold-core-amplitude",
          "exact_quote": "At a fixed $(t,x)$, the velocity measure $f_i(t,x,p)\\,\\mathrm dp$ is the pushforward of $$\\frac{K S(\\gamma(z_c-tp_3)/s)}\n          {s+t\\alpha\\gamma S(\\gamma(z_c-tp_3)/s)}\\,J(w)\\,\\mathrm dw,\\qquad\n \\bar p=d_s\\bar w,\\quad\n p_3=a_s^\\sigma(z_c-tp_3)+d_sw_3,\n \\label{cold:core-formula}$$ with the initial spatial masks evaluated at $x-tp$. For $t\\le L_s$, it is dominated by probability sheets with total amplitude $C/(t+s)$. The remainder $\\epsilon_s m_i\\,\\mathrm dp$ has sheet amplitude at most $$C L_sd_s/s^2\\le Cs^{15/2}\\le C/(t+s).\n \\label{cold:remainder-amplitude}$$ Thus the full upper bound $\\mathcal U_i\\,\\mathrm dp$ has sheet amplitude $C/(t+s)$. In particular, for every fixed $k\\ge0$, a main color satisfies $$\\int\\langle p\\rangle^k c_i(t,x,p)\\,\\mathrm dp\\le\\frac{C_k}{t+s},\\qquad\n \\int_{|p|>R}\\langle p\\rangle^k c_i(t,x,p)\\,\\mathrm dp\n       \\le\\frac{C_k e^{-c_kR^2}}{t+s}\\quad(R\\ge3).\n \\label{cold:moments}$$",

          "anchor": "stmt-early-cap-selection",
          "exact_quote": "For fixed $\\delta>0$, there are $\\bar s_0,T_0>0$, uniform in $N$ and the collision cutoff, with the following property. For each $0<s_0\\le\\bar s_0$, each $0<T\\le T_0$, and each finite approximation, a seed parameter $\\lambda\\in(0,L_0)$ gives a solution on $[0,T]$ satisfying $$\\label{early:cap}\n Z(t)\\le m_T(t+s_N)\\quad(0\\le t\\le T),\n \\qquad\n \\max_{0\\le t\\le T}\\frac{Z(t)}{m_T(t+s_N)}=1.$$ The dormant solution exists on a fixed initial interval and satisfies $$\\label{early:dormant-bound}\n Z(t)\\le C'_{A,\\delta}t^A.$$ The interval and constant in <a href=\"#early:dormant-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"early:dormant-bound\">[early:dormant-bound]</a> are independent of $N$, the collision cutoff, and sufficiently small $s_0$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-early-path-source",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $0<t_b<t_1$. There is a function $E_{t_b}(s_0)$ tending to zero as $s_0\\to0$ such that, for every finite evolution and every terminal time $t\\in[t_b,t_1]$ in its interval of existence, $$\\label{early:source-integral}\n \\left\\|\\int_{t_b}^t\n    \\mathcal S(s,x-(t-s)v,v)\\,\\mathrm ds\\right\\|_H\n \\le E_{t_b}(s_0).$$ This estimate is uniform in the hot seed, the cap horizon, the approximation index, and the collision cutoff.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }
      ],

      "output": "Small integrated source along every relevant free flight in essential norm.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-early-path-source"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-early-continuation",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $\\delta>0$ and $\\zeta\\in(0,1)$. There are $\\bar s_0>0$ and $T_0\\in(0,t_1)$, depending on $\\delta$ and $\\zeta$ and on the fixed jet parameters, such that for every $0<s_0\\le\\bar s_0$, every finite approximation, and every $0<T\\le T_0$, the dormant solution and every solution selected in Proposition <a href=\"#early:cap-selection\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"early:cap-selection\">22</a> continue to $t_1$ and satisfy $$\\label{early:small-hot}\n \\sup_{0\\le t\\le t_1}\\|h(t)\\|_H\\le\\zeta.$$ The choices are uniform in $N$, the collision cutoff, $R_\\mu$, and $T$. For dormant solutions, <a href=\"#early:dormant-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"early:dormant-bound\">[early:dormant-bound]</a> also holds on an initial interval independent of these indices, with the fixed exponent $A>p+1$ chosen in <a href=\"#early:exponents\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"early:exponents\">[early:exponents]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-global-flight-average",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-global-flight-average",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A:[0,\\infty)\\times(\\mathbb R^3/\\Lambda)\\to[0,\\infty)$ be measurable, write $A_s=A(s,\\cdot)$, and suppose $$\\sup_s\\|A_s\\|_\\infty\\le A_*,\\qquad\n \\sup_s\\|A_s\\|_1\\le a_*.$$ For a fixed bounded velocity set $V$ and $\\rho>0$, $$\\label{global:ball-average}\n \\int_V A_s(x-\\tau v)\\,\\mathrm dv\n \\le C_{V,\\rho}a_*\n \\qquad(\\tau\\ge\\rho),$$ uniformly in $x,s,\\tau$. If $A_*$ is fixed, the following quantities tend to zero as $a_*\\to0$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\sup_{t\\ge t_1,x}\n \\int_{t_1}^t e^{-\\kappa(t-s)}\n       \\int H(v)A_s(x-(t-s)v)\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm ds,\n       \\label{global:damped-average}\\\\\n &\\sup_{t\\ge t_1,x}\n \\int_0^{t_1}\\int H(v)A_s(x-(t-s)v)\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm ds,\n       \\label{global:early-average}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $\\kappa>0$ and $t_1>0$ are fixed. The supremum in $x$ is essential.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-global-flight-average"

          "anchor": "stmt-global-frequency",
          "exact_quote": "There is a constant $\\kappa>0$, depending only on the fixed Maxwellian $\\mu$ and $t_1$, with the following property. For each fixed $B>B_0+2$, if $\\varepsilon_E$ is sufficiently small depending on $B$, then every finite evolution satisfying <a href=\"#global:bootstrap\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"global:bootstrap\">[global:bootstrap]</a> obeys $$\\label{global:frequency-bound}\n \\nu_F^b(t,x,v)\\ge\\kappa\\qquad(t\\ge t_1).$$ The constant is uniform for $b\\ge1$ and for all approximation indices.",

After taking $\\varepsilon_E$, $\\zeta$, and $s_0$ sufficiently small depending on $B$, one has on that interval $$\\label{global:gain-bound}\n P^b(w,w)\\le C_5H,\n \\qquad C_5:=\\|P(\\mu,\\mu)\\|_H+1.$$ In particular, $C_5$ is independent of $B$, the approximation, and the cap horizon.       "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-global-control",
          "exact_quote": "The construction parameters can be fixed so that every dormant finite evolution and every cap solution with $0<T\\le T_0$ continues globally. There are constants $B<\\infty$ and $\\kappa>0$, independent of $N$, $b_N\\ge1$, and $T$, such that $$\\label{global:uniform-bound}\n \\|w(t)\\|_H\\le B,\\qquad \\nu_F^{b_N}(t,x,v)\\ge\\kappa,\n \\qquad t\\ge t_1.$$ Every active jet satisfies <a href=\"#global:cold-decay\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"global:cold-decay\">[global:cold-decay]</a> through its retirement, the total retirement increment is bounded by <a href=\"#global:retirement\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"global:retirement\">[global:retirement]</a>, and the hot component satisfies the small bound of <a href=\"#early:continuation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"early:continuation\">24</a> on $[0,t_1]$.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }

      "inputs": [

        "Damped cold estimates.",
        "Summable retirement increments."
      ],
      "method": "Use an integrable-coefficient Gronwall bound, choose parameters in dependency order, and restart strictly below the bootstrap threshold after retirements.",
      "output": "Global continuation of both finite families.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-global-control"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-comp-averaging",

For every smooth compactly supported velocity test $\\psi$, the averages $\\int\\psi(v)f_n(t,x,v)\\,\\mathrm dv$ are relatively compact in local $L^1_{t,x}$ wherever the hypotheses hold for all velocities in a neighborhood of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\psi$.       "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-global-limits",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-global-bootstrap",
        "step-velocity-averaging",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-global-limits",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the construction parameters supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:global-control\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:global-control\">28</a>, and let $b_N\\to\\infty$. Either the dormant family, or a family of cap solutions at one fixed horizon $T\\in(0,T_0]$, has a subsequence of total densities $F_N$ converging weakly in $L^1([0,\\tau]\\times\\Omega)$ for every $\\tau<\\infty$ to a nonnegative density $F$. This subsequence can be chosen so that $F_N(t)\\rightharpoonup F(t)$ in $L^1(\\Omega)$ at every $t\\ge0$, with uniformly small polynomially weighted velocity tails on each bounded time interval.*\n\n\n*The limit has initial datum $F_0$ and is a global entropy solution obeying local conservation in the sense of Definition <a href=\"#def:admissible\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:admissible\">1</a>. More precisely, $$\\label{comp:strong-properties}\n F\\in C([0,\\infty);L^1(\\Omega)),\\qquad\n \\int_0^\\tau\\!\\int_\\Omega\n \\langle v\\rangle^k\\bigl(F+Q^+(F,F)+Q^-(F,F)\\bigr)\n \\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm dt<\\infty\n \\quad(k\\ge0,\\ \\tau<\\infty).$$ Total mass, momentum, and energy are conserved at every time. The common early-time pointwise majorant of the dormant approximations also bounds any dormant limit.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-global-limit",
      "inputs": [
        "Global uniform control.",
        "Convergent initial data.",
        "Uniformly integrable collision products."
      ],
      "method": "Discard small annular scales, identify pair-product limits using velocity averaging, and pass entropy and local balances with weighted collision integrability.",

      "proof_anchor": "sec:compactness"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-blowup-limit",
      "depends_on": [

        "step-core-amplitudes",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-blowup-limit",
          "exact_quote": "Every sequence <a href=\"#eq:rescaling\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rescaling\">[eq:rescaling]</a> has a subsequence for which $h_{r_N}\\rightharpoonup g$ in local spacetime $L^1$ on $(0,U)\\times\\mathbb R^3_x\\times\\mathbb R^3_v$, where $$\\label{eq:limit-equation}\n0\\leq g(t,x,v)\\leq(t+l)H(v),\\qquad\nD_tg=2P(B,g)-g\\nu_B.$$ Here $B$ is <a href=\"#eq:background\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:background\">[eq:background]</a>; its gain and loss are defined by integration against the velocity measure. Both terms on the right are locally integrable, with a velocity majorant uniform in position on each compact positive-time interval. Compactly supported velocity averages of $h_{r_N}$ converge strongly in local $L^1_{t,x}$. The limit has a locally strongly continuous $L^1$ representative at positive times and obeys the integrating-factor formula <a href=\"#dil:mild\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dil:mild\">[dil:mild]</a> below.*\n\n\n*If $l>0$, pass also to a limit $\\lambda_N\\to\\lambda_0$ of the seed parameters. The conclusion and its local continuity extend to time zero, with $$\\label{dil:initial-trace}\ng(0,x,v)=\\lambda_0 l\\,\\chi(x/l,v).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }
      ],

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-dil-free-core-comparison",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-dilated-linear-limit",

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-dil-free-core-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $N$, define the velocity measure $$A_N(t,x,\\,\\mathrm dp)\n   =r_N\\sum_{i:\\,j\\le N}f_i(r_Nt,r_Nx,p)\\,\\mathrm dp,\n       \\qquad 0\\le t\\le1.$$ This definition uses free transport of the initial colors and is meaningful even if $t>U_N$. At each position, at most one summand is nonzero. Its velocity moments and normalized gain obey $$\\int\\langle p\\rangle^k A_N(t,x,\\,\\mathrm dp)\n      \\le\\frac{C_k}{t+l_N},\\qquad\n P(A_N,H)\\le\\frac{C}{t+l_N}H.$$ There are numbers $\\varepsilon_N\\downarrow0$ such that, on $0\\le t\\le\\min(1,U_N)$, the hot component obeys the pathwise upper comparison $$\\label{dil:positive-comparison}\n D_th_{r_N}\\le2P(A_N,h_{r_N})+\\varepsilon_NH.$$ The bounds and comparison hold on full-measure endpoint sets at each time section, with representatives given by their path formulas.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }

      "id": "step-free-core-comparison",
      "inputs": [

      "method": "Replace actual cold colors by free-core upper bounds and discard nonnegative losses.",
      "output": "A positive free-core comparison valid up to the cap.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-dil-free-core-comparison"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-nc-core-kernels",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-free-core-comparison",
        "step-parameter-sheets",
        "step-sheet-tightness"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nc-core-kernels",
          "exact_quote": "There are nonnegative velocity measures $A_n(t,x,\\,\\mathrm dp)$ and numbers $\\varepsilon_n\\to0$ with the following properties. After discarding finitely many terms, the measures $A_n$ are defined from free transport on the whole interval $0\\le t\\le1$, independently of the cap interval $[0,U_n]$. The bounds on $A_n$ below hold on that whole interval; comparison with $h_n$ is used only while the cap bound holds.*\n\n\n1.  *On $0<t<\\min(1,U_n)$, along almost every free characteristic, $$\\label{nc:positive-equation}\n     D_th_n\\le 2P(A_n,h_n)+\\varepsilon_n H .$$ At each point $A_n$ contains at most one main free core. Its dilated scale $\\varrho=s/r_n$ satisfies $|y|\\asymp\\varrho$, with coordinates measured from its periodic copy, and $$\\frac{2P(A_n,H)}{H}\\le\\frac{C}{t+\\ell_n}.$$*\n\n\n2.  *Before spatial masks are imposed, a core of scale $\\varrho=s/r_n$ and sign $\\sigma$ is the pushforward of $$\\label{nc:core-parameters}\n     \\frac{K S\\bigl(\\gamma(z-tp_3)/\\varrho\\bigr)}\n          {\\varrho+t\\alpha\\gamma S\\bigl(\\gamma(z-tp_3)/\\varrho\\bigr)}\n     J(w)\\,\\mathrm dw ,\n     \\qquad\n     \\bar p=d_s\\bar w,\\quad\n     p_3=\\sigma+\\alpha\\tanh\\bigl(\\gamma(z-tp_3)/\\varrho\\bigr)+d_sw_3 .$$ The actual masks are measurable factors between zero and one. The density in <a href=\"#nc:core-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"nc:core-parameters\">[nc:core-parameters]</a> is at most $C(t+\\varrho)^{-1}J(w)$.*\n\n\n3.  *For $t\\ge a>0$, the normalized gain measure $$\\label{nc:normalized-kernel}\n     \\mathcal K_n(t,x,v;\\,\\mathrm dp\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\xi)\n     =\n     4A_n(t,x,\\,\\mathrm dp)\\,\n     \\delta\\bigl((v-p)\\cdot(\\xi-v)\\bigr)\n     \\frac{H(\\xi)}{H(v)}\\,\\mathrm d\\xi$$ has total mass at most $C/a$. Uniformly in its base point, the contributions from large $|p|+|\\xi-v|$, or from large retained Gaussian parameters in a product-sheet majorant, tend to zero. Its total mass tends uniformly to zero as $|v|\\to\\infty$.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nc-endpoint-tightness",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $a>0$, and let $t_n\\in[a,1]$. In a five-fold integral $(\\mathcal V_n^5F_n)(t_n,x_n,v_n)$, assume $0\\le F_n\\le2$. For every prescribed error, one may restrict all $|p_i|+|v_i-v_{i-1}|$ and sheet parameters to fixed bounded sets at that error, uniformly in $n,t_n,x_n,v_n$. Moreover:*\n\n1.  *if $|v_n|\\to\\infty$, the whole integral tends to zero;*\n\n2.  *after retaining bounded endpoint velocities, an endpoint escaping every bounded neighborhood of every dilated periodic origin also has a vanishing integral.*\n\n*Consequently any sequence along which the five-fold integral has a positive lower bound has, after periodic translations and subselection, convergent endpoint positions and velocities.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-axis-kernel-estimates",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-geometric-slices",
        "step-sheet-tightness"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-axis-kernel-estimates",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\leq L<\\infty$. The following conclusions hold uniformly for $c\\in\\mathcal S_L$ and for outgoing velocities off the axis.*\n\n\n\n1.  *For every fixed $M,\\delta>0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n     &\\lim_{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0}\n     \\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_{\\substack{|v|\\leq M\\\\0<|\\bar v|\\leq\\varepsilon}}\n     \\mathcal K_c(v;|\\bar\\xi|\\geq\\delta)=0,\n     \\label{ker:axis-input}\\\\\n     &\\lim_{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0}\n     \\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_{\\substack{|v|\\leq M\\\\0<|\\bar v|\\leq\\varepsilon}}\n     \\mathcal K_c(v;|v_3-p_3|\\geq\\delta)=0.\n     \\label{ker:axis-mismatch}\n    \\end{aligned}$$*\n\n\n\n2.  *The axial input marginals are uniformly non-atomic. More precisely, for each $R\\geq1$ and each interval $I$ of length $0<\\ell\\leq1$, $$\\label{ker:axial-interval}\n     \\mathcal K_c(v;|p|+|\\xi-v|\\leq R,\\ \\xi_3\\in I)\n     \\leq C_{q,m,L,R}\\bigl(\\ell+\\ell^{2-q}\\bigr).$$ In combination with <a href=\"#ker:uniform-tail\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ker:uniform-tail\">[ker:uniform-tail]</a>, this gives $$\\label{ker:axial-modulus}\n     \\lim_{\\ell\\downarrow0}\\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L}\\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_v\n     \\sup_{|I|\\leq\\ell}\\mathcal K_c(v;\\xi_3\\in I)=0.$$*\n\n\n3.  *Let $\\mathcal S_L(\\varepsilon)$ denote the subclass whose elementary transverse widths are at most $\\varepsilon$. For every $M<\\infty$ and $r_0>0$, $$\\label{ker:off-axis-small-input}\n     \\lim_{\\delta\\downarrow0}\\limsup_{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0}\n     \\sup_{c\\in\\mathcal S_L(\\varepsilon)}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{ess\\,sup}}_{\\substack{|v|\\leq M\\\\|\\bar v|\\geq r_0}}\n     \\mathcal K_c(v;|\\bar\\xi|<\\delta)=0.$$*\n\n*All these conclusions hold under uniformly bounded sheet amplitudes.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-axis-kernel-estimates",

        "Normalized kernel tightness."

      "method": "Use transverse line and axial delta integration, axial-slice moduli, and shrinking-width Gaussian tail deletion.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nc-characteristic-matching",
          "exact_quote": "On every compact set with $t\\ge a>0$, the following statements hold uniformly.*\n\n\n1.  *If $a\\le t_2\\le t_1$, $\\Delta=t_1-t_2$, and $z_2=z_1-\\Delta w$, then $$\\label{nc:matching-inequality}\n     |w-u_{\\varrho,\\sigma}(t_1,z_1)|\n     \\le |w-u_{\\varrho,\\sigma}(t_2,z_2)|.$$*\n\n\n2.  *As $\\varrho\\downarrow0$, the distance of $u_{\\varrho,\\sigma}(t,z)$ to $$\\{z/t,\\ \\sigma-\\alpha,\\ \\sigma+\\alpha\\}$$ tends to zero uniformly. For the perturbed axial velocity in <a href=\"#nc:core-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"nc:core-parameters\">[nc:core-parameters]</a>, $$\\label{nc:thermal-speed-error}\n     |p_3-u_{\\varrho,\\sigma}(t,z)|\\le d_s|w_3|.$$",

          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ne0$, $e=n/|n|$, and let $\\zeta$ be a bounded nonnegative function supported in $[-1,1]$ with integral one. For every nonnegative measurable $F$, $$\\label{nc:plane-identity}\n \\int_{n^\\perp} F(k)\\frac{\\,\\mathrm dA(k)}{|n|}\n =\n \\int_{\\mathbb R^3}F(n\\times L)|n|\\zeta(L\\cdot e)\\,\\mathrm dL .$$ When $\\delta\\le|n|\\le R$ and the plane integral is restricted to $|k|\\le R_1$, the corresponding parameter measure $\\mathbf 1_{\\{|n\\times L|\\le R_1\\}}|n|\\zeta(L\\cdot e)\\,\\mathrm dL$ is supported in $$|L|\\le\\sqrt{(R_1/\\delta)^2+1}$$ and has density at most $R\\|\\zeta\\|_\\infty$. This bound concerns the parameter measure before multiplication by $F(n\\times L)$. The identity can be applied successively when each normal is a measurable function of parameters chosen at earlier gains.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-same-datum-nonuniqueness**: The constructed datum admits distinct global entropy solutions with exact local conservation and strong continuity in time.
- **invariant-finite-approximation-uniqueness**: Each fixed finite colored approximation has a unique nonnegative local mild solution in the bounded weighted norm class, and continuation requires that norm to remain bounded.
- **invariant-small-mass-under-domination**: Small velocity mass controls the normalized mixed gain only within a fixed weighted order interval; the estimate also holds for the cutoff gain.
- **invariant-history-lower-bound**: Integrating an allowed collection of positive finite collision histories yields a measure dominated by the solution at the terminal time.
- **invariant-nonzero-limit-finite-horizon**: With the selected construction parameters, a nonzero dilated limit satisfying the cap cannot persist for all positive dilated times.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-universal-nonuniqueness**: Conclude that every finite-entropy initial datum admits distinct solutions. -> The existential construction does not establish nonuniqueness for arbitrary data or for the stronger uniqueness classes discussed in the introduction.
- **anti-finite-approximation-branch-certificate**: Treat distinct computed finite approximations as a certificate of distinct entropy solutions with the same initial datum. -> The finite colored evolution has uniqueness in its stated class; separation of the limiting solutions requires compactness, nonvanishing, and the dilation comparison.
- **anti-global-inequalities-as-local-balances**: Accept global energy and entropy inequalities as verification of exact local momentum and energy conservation. -> Global inequalities do not provide the required distributional local identities, energy flux integrability, or initial traces.
- **anti-mass-only-gain-control**: Apply the small-mass gain estimate to an arbitrary density having small velocity mass. -> Removing domination by the fixed weighted envelope removes an explicit hypothesis of the estimate.
- **anti-single-gain-axial-decay**: Assume a normalized gain necessarily vanishes as the outgoing velocity approaches the axis. -> The sharp axial endpoint example has a strictly positive limiting normalized gain.
- **anti-reversed-history-domination**: Use a restricted collection of branching histories as an upper bound for the transport solution. -> Discarding positive histories yields a lower bound; reversing domination invalidates subsequent estimates.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result asserts existence and separation of global solutions of a continuum equation. The global-limit proposition requires weak subsequence extraction as the collision cutoff diverges, and separation uses shrinking horizons, nonconcentration, and a contradiction involving the dilated horizon. These are not a finite constructive input-output contract. Finite checks of collision identities or branching bounds would certify only auxiliary instances, not existence or separation of the global entropy solutions.

          }

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The existence of distinct entropy solutions does not supply an executable procedure for computing either branch or deciding which branch a discretization selects. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The dilute-gas collision model and concentrating initial densities do not provide a finite particle simulation or a game collision-response algorithm. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The small-mass gain estimate supplies an analytical bound under a fixed domination hypothesis, without a procedure for obtaining its constant from finite input. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Cap selection uses continuous dependence and an endpoint argument to establish a seed parameter's existence; it supplies neither a finite search algorithm nor a termination bound. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sheet class organizes probability measures for analytical domination, including mixtures and axial atoms; no finite representation or supported data operations are specified. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The collision-plane representation identifies a continuous integration geometry but gives no accelerator construction, arithmetic precision contract, or hardware resource bound. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The irrational lattice orientation supplies geometric separation of periodic jets, without a cryptographic primitive, adversary model, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The collision weight is an analytical majorant for singular velocity distributions; the paper does not construct a learned model, training objective, or statistical generalization guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The continuation proof orders construction parameters to satisfy analytical inequalities, rather than optimizing an objective over an explicitly computable feasible set. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The colored decomposition and retirement transfers regroup components of a kinetic density; they do not specify communication, replica consistency, or failure recovery. |
| other (Mathematical kinetic theory) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs a periodic gas datum from annular jets and a surrounding bath, then obtains distinct global entropy evolutions through dormant and cap-selected approximation families. This supports analytical study of solution selection; finite approximations alone do not establish the limiting nonuniqueness. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-existential-datum** (counterexample_scope): Nonuniqueness concerns a specially constructed datum; it is not asserted for every datum satisfying the integrability assumptions. Blocked use: ['Inferring generic nonuniqueness from finite mass, energy, and absolute entropy alone.']
- **limit-periodic-hard-sphere-setting** (hypothesis): The construction uses the periodic hard-sphere collision equation. Other collision laws and spatial boundary conditions require separate arguments. Blocked use: ['Transferring the conclusion directly to a different collision potential or a domain with physical walls.']
- **limit-stronger-uniqueness-classes** (counterexample_scope): The concentrating datum is distinguished from the stronger regularity and amplitude classes in which uniqueness is cited. Blocked use: ['Claiming that the construction contradicts uniqueness for bounded Maxwellian classes or small regular perturbations.']
- **limit-exact-local-admissibility** (hypothesis): Global inequalities alone do not establish the required local momentum and energy identities, energy flux integrability, or initial traces. Blocked use: ['Treating global entropy and energy checks as sufficient verification of the locally conservative solution class.']
- **limit-weight-exponent-regime** (hypothesis): The velocity majorant requires the stated transverse singularity regime. Integrability of the majorant and convergence of the plane integrals use different sides of that regime. Blocked use: ['Extending the weighted estimates to an endpoint exponent without proving the corresponding integrability and plane estimates.']
- **limit-essential-axis-estimates** (edge_case): The weighted supremum and kernel assertions concern essential estimates away from the velocity axis; they do not assign meaningful pointwise values on that axis. Blocked use: ['Using values assigned on the velocity axis to contradict an essential bound or to define an exceptional collision contribution.']
- **limit-sheet-domination** (hypothesis): Uniform sheet bounds require the specified transverse Gaussian structure and a controlled axial exponential moment. Absence of an axial density bound does not remove these conditions. Blocked use: ['Applying the sheet estimates to arbitrary velocity measures without establishing sheet domination and its amplitude.']
- **limit-parameter-law-and-mixtures** (hypothesis): Measurable axial compression is permitted under the Gaussian parameter majorant and the stated growth and width bounds. Positive mixing changes the quantitative amplitude according to its total mass. Blocked use: ['Treating arbitrary parameter tails, unrestricted axial growth, or unbounded mixing mass as covered by the parameter-sheet estimate.']
- **limit-small-mass-order-interval** (hypothesis): Small velocity mass controls the gain only when the density remains in a fixed weighted order interval; the constant depends on that interval and the requested tolerance. Blocked use: ['Invoking the small-mass estimate for a density whose weighted domination has not been established.']
- **limit-fixed-colored-approximation** (approximation): Each fixed finite colored cutoff system has uniqueness in its bounded weighted-norm mild class. This is distinct from the nonuniqueness of the limiting entropy solutions. Blocked use: ['Using the main theorem to assert nonuniqueness at fixed truncation, cutoff, horizon, and seed.']
- **limit-active-color-lifetime** (hypothesis): Disjointness and the active-color comparison are restricted to the prescribed retirement intervals and sufficiently small construction scale. Exponential decay additionally requires a positive collision-frequency lower bound. Blocked use: ['Keeping all colors separate indefinitely under the same geometry estimates.', 'Assuming damped cold decay before establishing its frequency hypothesis.']
- **limit-cap-selection-effectivity** (nonconstructive): The seed parameter is selected through the endpoint of an open parameter set and continuous dependence. The argument supplies no finite certified procedure for finding an exactly attaining seed. Blocked use: ['Presenting numerical seed tuning as a certified implementation of cap attainment.']
- **limit-global-bootstrap-parameter-order** (hypothesis): Global continuation requires the ordered parameter choices that produce small entropy distance, a small hot component, small tube volume, and summable retirement increments. The provisional weighted bound is a bootstrap hypothesis until improved. Blocked use: ['Applying the uniform continuation conclusion to arbitrary bath, seed, or jet parameters.', 'Using the positive collision frequency without the required entropy tolerance.']
- **limit-spatial-entropy-versus-pointwise-control** (hypothesis): Small relative entropy initially supplies spatially integrated control, not pointwise smallness of the entropy distance. The continuation proof obtains the needed pointwise information through transport averaging. Blocked use: ['Replacing the free-flight averaging argument with an unsupported pointwise entropy-distance bound.']
- **limit-compactness-and-collision-products** (nonconstructive): Global solutions are obtained by subsequence extraction. Identification of the collision terms requires uniform integrability of same-position pair products and weighted velocity-tail control, beyond weak convergence of densities alone. Blocked use: ['Claiming convergence of the entire approximation family or a numerical convergence rate.', 'Passing quadratic collision products using weak density convergence without the additional compactness.']
- **limit-positive-time-dilated-traces** (edge_case): The linear dilated limit is asserted on the open positive-time interval. Its extension to the initial time requires a positive lower mesh endpoint and a subsequential limit of seed parameters. Blocked use: ['Assigning the stated initial trace to a dilated limit with a vanishing lower mesh endpoint.']
- **limit-single-gain-axial-endpoint** (edge_case): A single normalized gain need not vanish near the velocity axis. The axial nonconcentration argument uses successive gains and axial interval control. Blocked use: ['Replacing the successive-gain axial argument by an assumed single-gain decay estimate.']
- **limit-pullback-rank-and-localization** (hypothesis): The pullback estimate requires local convergence of the maps and full limiting differential rank almost everywhere, together with bounded densities and compact supports. Its collision application requires nonaxial endpoints and positive retained scales. Blocked use: ['Applying pullback convergence at arbitrary degenerate endpoints or without the stated compactness and rank conditions.']
- **limit-history-lower-bound-direction** (hypothesis): Restricted positive collision histories produce a measure dominated by the terminal solution. They do not give an upper bound on that solution. Blocked use: ['Reversing history domination when estimating solution mass.', 'Omitting local integrability or bounded compact-set rate hypotheses from the history argument.']
- **limit-measurable-medium-closeness** (hypothesis): The robust branching block allows measurable coefficients, but requires uniform closeness to the reference beams throughout the prescribed bounded-speed excursions. Blocked use: ['Replacing excursion-wise closeness with a spatial average or checking the coefficients only along a reference path.']
- **limit-dilated-horizon-not-physical-breakdown** (formal_scope): The finite-horizon conclusion concerns a nonzero linear dilated profile under its cap. The physical entropy solutions constructed by the paper remain global. Blocked use: ['Interpreting finiteness of the dilated horizon as finite-time breakdown of the original global solutions.']
- **limit-separation-without-uniform-rate** (nonconstructive): Separation uses a horizon-dependent diagonal extraction and comparability of dilation scales obtained from the finite dilated horizon. It supplies no convergence rate uniform in the shrinking horizon. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite discretization or a fixed numerical tolerance as a certificate that the limiting branches are distinct.']

For every $\\beta\\in C^1([0,\\infty))$ with $\\beta(0)=0$ and $|\\beta'(z)|\\le C_\\beta/(1+z)$, and every smooth $\\varphi$ periodic in $x$ and compactly supported in $[0,\\infty)\\times\\mathbb R^3_v$, $$\\label{eq:renormalized}\n    \\begin{split}\n     &\\int_0^\\infty\\!\\!\\int_\\Omega\n     \\left[M\\beta(G)D_t\\varphi+\\beta'(G)Q(F,F)\\varphi\\right]\n                      \\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm dt\\\\\n     &\\hspace{15mm}\n     +\\int_\\Omega M\\beta(F_0/M)\\varphi(0,x,v)\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv=0.\n    \\end{split}$$\n\n3.  Total momentum is conserved. At every $t\\ge0$, $$\\int_\\Omega |v|^2F(t)\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\le\\int_\\Omega |v|^2F_0\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv,\n     \\qquad\n     \\mathcal H_M(F(t))+\\int_0^t\\mathcal D(F(s))\\,\\mathrm ds\n          \\le\\mathcal H_M(F_0).$$\n\n\n4.  For every finite $T$, $$\\int_0^T\\!\\!\\int_\\Omega (1+|v|^3)F\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm dt<\\infty.$$ For every $\\psi\\in\\{1,v_1,v_2,v_3,|v|^2/2\\}$ and $\\phi\\in C_c^\\infty([0,\\infty)\\times\\mathbb T^3)$, $$\\label{eq:local-balances}\n     \\int_0^\\infty\\!\\!\\int_\\Omega F\\psi D_t\\phi\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm dt\n       +\\int_\\Omega F_0\\psi\\phi(0,x)\\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv=0.$$",

          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal O\\subset\\mathbb R^k$ be open, $k\\ge d$, and suppose $\\Phi_n,\\Phi:\\mathcal O\\to\\mathbb R^d$ satisfy $\\Phi_n\\to\\Phi$ in $C^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ and $\\operatorname{rank}D\\Phi=d$ almost everywhere. Let $K\\Subset\\mathcal O$ and $B\\subset\\mathbb R^d$ be compact. Let $a_n$ be measurable, supported in $K$, with $|a_n|\\le A$. Let $F_n$ be measurable, supported in $B$, with $|F_n|\\le M$, and converging to zero in measure. Then $$\\label{nc:pullback-conclusion}\n \\int_{\\mathcal O}|F_n(\\Phi_n(q))a_n(q)|\\,\\mathrm dq\\longrightarrow0 .$$ No convergence or continuity of the densities $a_n$ is required.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-five-gain-rank",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a nonaxial endpoint velocity $v_0$, an endpoint $(t_0,x_0)$ with $t_0>0$, five positive scales $\\varrho_i$, and five signs $\\sigma_i$. Given positive times $t_i$ and $L_i\\in\\mathbb R^3$, define recursively $$\\label{nc:rank-map}\n \\begin{split}\n x_i&=x_{i-1}-(t_{i-1}-t_i)v_{i-1},\\\\\n p_i&=u_{\\varrho_i,\\sigma_i}(t_i,z_i)e_3,\\qquad\n n_i=v_{i-1}-p_i,\\\\\n k_i&=n_i\\times L_i,\\qquad v_i=v_{i-1}+k_i ,\n \\qquad i=1,\\ldots,5 .\n \\end{split}$$ The map $$\\Phi:(t_1,\\ldots,t_5,L_1,\\ldots,L_5)\n       \\longmapsto(t_5,x_5,v_5)$$ is real analytic on the connected open set $(0,\\infty)^5\\times\\mathbb R^{15}$, and has rank seven almost everywhere. In particular it is a submersion almost everywhere on the ordered region $0<t_5<\\cdots<t_1<t_0$.*\n\n*For convergent endpoint sequences with a nonaxial limiting velocity, convergent positive scale sequences, and the finite-temperature core formulas <a href=\"#nc:core-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"nc:core-parameters\">[nc:core-parameters]</a>, the analogous maps converge in $C^1$ on compact parameter sets to $\\Phi$. Bounded measurable parameter densities supported in a common compact set therefore satisfy Lemma <a href=\"#nc:uniform-pullback\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"nc:uniform-pullback\">41</a>, including after any measurable spatial, velocity, or time restrictions.",

        }

Suppose a measurable medium obeys, throughout every speed-$D$ excursion from a specified starting point over a duration $A\\in I_L$, $$\\label{br:medium-closeness}\n|b_\\sigma-1|\\le\\varepsilon_*,\n\\qquad |w_\\sigma-\\sigma e_3|\\le\\varepsilon_*\n\\quad(\\sigma=\\pm1).$$ From each starting velocity in $E$, histories with between $1$ and $J_{\\mathrm{br}}$ collisions, speed at most $D$ on every free flight, every postcollision velocity and the final velocity strictly inside $E$, and $$\\label{br:robust-drift}\n|Z_A-\\varkappa Ae_1|<d\\varkappa A$$ have total weight greater than 3. The initial free flight may have speed $D$. No continuity of the medium is needed.",
          "source_id": "PM-363.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-measurable-medium-closeness",
      "kind": "hypothesis",

    },
    {

      ],
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-dilated-horizon",
          "exact_quote": "There are fixed choices $$K\\ge1,\\qquad 0<\\eta\\le\\log2,\\qquad\n0<\\alpha\\le\\tfrac14,\\qquad 0<\\gamma\\le1$$ with the following property. For every allowed geometric mesh with lower endpoint $l\\in[0,1]$, the only nonnegative density solving <a href=\"#eq:limit-equation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:limit-equation\">[eq:limit-equation]</a> on $0<t<\\infty$, with locally integrable gain and loss, the positive-time mild formula <a href=\"#dil:mild\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dil:mild\">[dil:mild]</a>, and the bound $g(t,x,v)\\le(t+l)H(v)$, is zero. Consequently every nonzero limit in Propositions <a href=\"#prop:blowup-limit\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:blowup-limit\">31</a> and <a href=\"#prop:nonzero-limit\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:nonzero-limit\">33</a> has $U<\\infty$. Moreover, for every finite $T$ and every $k\\ge0$, they satisfy $$\\label{eq:strong-collision-integrability}\n \\int_0^T\\!\\!\\int_\\Omega\n \\langle v\\rangle^k\\bigl[Q^+(F,F)+Q^-(F,F)\\bigr]\n                       \\,\\mathrm dx\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm dt<\\infty,\n \\qquad \\langle v\\rangle=(1+|v|^2)^{1/2}.$$",

<!-- PM:END pm.formal.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove nonuniqueness for the three-dimensional periodic hard-sphere Boltzmann equation among global entropy solutions satisfying exact local conservation of mass, momentum, and kinetic energy. We construct one nonnegative initial density with bounded velocity support and finite mass, energy, and absolute entropy that gives rise to two distinct such solutions. Both are strongly continuous in <i>L</i><sup>1</sup>, and their collision gain and loss terms are integrable with every polynomial velocity weight on every bounded time interval.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The Boltzmann equation describes the transport and binary collisions of a dilute gas. Its collision law conserves mass, momentum, and kinetic energy at each spatial point, while its entropy decreases. A weak solution theory must retain enough of these properties to distinguish the equation from a balance law with an unidentified collision defect. This paper constructs two global solutions with the same initial datum for which all five local collision-invariant balances hold exactly.

The connection between binary collisions and entropy dissipation goes back to Boltzmann’s kinetic theory (Boltzmann 1872) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Boltzmann1872](#ref-Boltzmann1872)]. For the spatially inhomogeneous Cauchy problem, DiPerna and Lions established the large-data theory of global existence and weak stability for renormalized solutions (DiPerna and Lions 1989) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[DiPernaLions1989](#ref-DiPernaLions1989)]. On periodic space the initial assumptions are finite mass, energy, and entropy, and the solutions obey the entropy-production inequality (DiPerna and Lions 1991, Theorem 1 and Remark 2) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[DiPernaLions1991](#ref-DiPernaLions1991)]. Renormalization addresses a basic difficulty: these bounds alone need not make the quadratic collision term locally integrable.

Exact conservation is an additional issue at this level of regularity. The periodic formulation in (Levermore and Masmoudi 2010, Theorem 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[LevermoreMasmoudi2010](#ref-LevermoreMasmoudi2010)] gives local mass conservation, but allows a nonnegative matrix-valued defect in the momentum balance and a corresponding defect in total energy. It does not automatically give exact local momentum and energy conservation. The present result concerns solutions that obey both of these local identities, with the energy flux integrable and with the prescribed initial traces. Their separate collision gains and losses are integrable with every polynomial velocity weight on each finite time interval.

Uniqueness is available under stronger control. Kaniel and Shinbrot proved local existence and uniqueness in classes bounded by a multiple of a Maxwellian (Kaniel and Shinbrot 1978) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[KanielShinbrot1978](#ref-KanielShinbrot1978)]. Ukai obtained global existence and uniqueness for small periodic perturbations of a Maxwellian in weighted spatial Sobolev spaces (Ukai 1974, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Ukai1974](#ref-Ukai1974)]. Duan, Huang, Wang, and Yang later allowed large but bounded amplitudes on the torus or in the whole space, assuming small relative entropy and a small spatially integrated velocity supremum of the perturbation (Duan et al. 2017, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[DuanHuangWangYang2017](#ref-DuanHuangWangYang2017)]. Our datum instead has unbounded amplitudes at shrinking spatial scales. The small relative entropy used in its construction therefore does not place it in these uniqueness classes.

For the spatially homogeneous hard-sphere equation, finite mass and energy suffice for uniqueness within the conservative class (Mischler and Wennberg 1999, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[MischlerWennberg1999](#ref-MischlerWennberg1999)]. Wennberg’s homogeneous nonuniqueness examples for hard potentials allow increasing energy (Wennberg 1999) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Wennberg1999](#ref-Wennberg1999)].
